Chapter 231. Fire Prevention 
 
Article XIII. Fireworks and Pyrotechnics Displays 
 
§ 231-37. Permit requirements. 
 
  
C. Pursuant to New York Penal Law § 405.00, the Town Board hereby 
designates the Fire Marshal as the Town's permit authority for fireworks 
and pyrotechnics displays. All applications shall require the approval of the 
Fire Marshal and consultation with the Fire Chief of the district in which the 
display will take place. Upon determining that an application complies with 
the requirements of this chapter and all applicable federal, state and local 
laws, rules and regulations, the Fire Marshal shall approve, approve with 
conditions, or deny the application and shall issue the permit, if approved. 
  
D. Appeals. Any person aggrieved by a determination of the Fire Marshal 
under this section may appeal such determination to the Town Board within 
30 days of the date of the determination.

Chapter 115. Procurement Policy 
§ 115-1. Purpose. 
The purpose of this Procurement Policy is to ensure that sound business judgment is utilized in all 
procurement transactions and that supplies, equipment, contracts, and services are obtained 
efficiently and in compliance with applicable laws and to ensure that all procurement transactions will 
be conducted in a manner that provides full and open competition and prudent and economical use of 
public money. In addition, this Procurement Policy seeks to ensure that the same sound business 
judgment used for the purchase of personal property (i.e., supplies and equipment) is used for the 
evaluation of personal property and/or assets with respect to its usefulness to the Town now or in the 
foreseeable future and cost effective and efficient procedures for disposition of the personal property 
and/or assets deemed no longer of public use to the Town. 
§ 115-2. Guidelines. 
A. Guideline 1. Every prospective purchase of goods or services shall be evaluated to determine the 
applicability of General Municipal Law § 103 inclusive of all future statutory amendments, deemed 
to be incorporated by reference, as if recited in its entirety herein. Every Town officer, board, 
department head or other personnel with the requisite purchasing authority (hereinafter 
"purchaser") shall estimate the cumulative amount of the items of supply or equipment needed in 
a given fiscal year. The information gathered and conclusions reached shall be documented and 
kept with the file or other documentation supporting the purchase activity. 
 
B. Guideline 2. All purchases of supplies or equipment which will exceed $20,000 in the fiscal year 
or public works contracts over $35,000 shall be formally bid pursuant to General Municipal Law 
§ 103 inclusive of all future statutory amendments, deemed to be incorporated by reference, as if 
recited in its entirety herein. In addition, New York State Finance Law § 139-L, effective January 
1, 2019, requires that all competitive bids for goods sold or services to be performed contain a 
statement subscribed by the bidder and affirmed by such bidder that the "bidder has and has 
implemented a written policy addressing sexual harassment prevention in the workplace and 
provides annual sexual harassment prevention training to all of its employees" . . . and "[s]uch 
policy shall, at a minimum, meet the requirements of section 201-g of the labor law" [New York 
State Finance Law § 139-L(a)]. In addition to compliance with General Municipal Law and 
Finance Law recited above, public works contracts involving construction contracts, as defined in 
Labor Law § 816-b, in excess of $250,000 and/or 100,000 square feet, whichever is less, shall 
require the bidder/contractor and subcontractors on such construction contracts to meet 
requirements set forth in Guideline 5, except for the following purchase of goods or public works 
contracts: purchase of goods or public works contracts identified in Guideline 6(1), (2)(a) and (b), 
(3), (4) and (6); public works contracts for a specific trade which is not included among the list 
maintained by the New York State Commissioner of Labor or such trade is classified as 
miscellaneous or general for such apprenticeship agreement at the time of opening of the bid; any 
construction contract utilizing federal, state, county or other funding assistance, including 
donation(s) to the extent the funding assistance precludes application of this section. 
 
C. Guideline 3. 
 
(1)  All estimated purchases of less than $20,000 but greater than $3,000 shall require a written 
request for a proposal ("RFP") and written/fax quotes from at least three comparative vendors; 
less than $3,000 but greater than $1,000 require an oral request for proposal for the goods, 
equipment or supplies and oral/fax quotes from at least two vendors and purchaser shall 
Page 12 of 307
maintain a record of responses whether they are received by phone or in writing detailing 
date/time, name of individual offering quote, and remarks detailing quote; less than $1,000 are 
left to the discretion of the purchaser. 
 
(2)  All estimated public works contracts of less than $35,000 but greater than $10,000 require a 
written request for proposal and fax/proposals from at least three comparable contractors; less 
than $10,000 but greater than $1,000 require a written request for proposal and fax/proposals 
from at least two contractors and purchaser shall maintain a record of responses whether they 
are received by phone or in writing detailing date/time, name of individual offering quote, and 
remarks detailing quote; less than $1,000 are left to the discretion of the purchaser. 
 
(3)  All written RFP shall describe the desired goods, quantity and details regarding delivery, 
including mode of delivery and time parameters for delivery. The purchaser shall compile a list of 
all vendors from whom written/fax/oral quotes have been requested and the written/fax/oral 
quotes offered. 
 
(4)  All information gathered in complying with the procedures of this guideline shall be preserved 
and filed with the documentation supporting the subsequent purchase or public works contract. 
All responses to the RFP shall be kept confidential until such time as the contract for work or 
goods is awarded and thereafter all documents responsive to the RFP shall be subject to 
disclosure pursuant to the applicable provisions of the Public Officers Law. In addition, while 
the recent amendments to New York State Finance Law § 139-L(b), effective January 1, 2019, 
do not require the certification set forth above for bids that do not require competitive bid [see 
Guideline 3 subsections (1), (2) and (3) and Guidelines 5 and 6 set forth herein] and instead 
recite that the department, agency, or official has discretion to require the certification, all 
department heads shall consider and as appropriate require the Finance Law § 139(a) 
certification. 
 
D.  Guideline 4. All purchasers shall make a good-faith effort to obtain the required number of 
proposals or quotations. If the purchaser is unable to obtain the required number of proposals or 
quotations, the purchaser shall document the attempt made at obtaining the proposals. In no 
event shall the inability to obtain the proposals or quotes be a bar to the procurement. 
 
E. Guideline 5. The lowest responsible proposal or quote shall be awarded the purchase or public 
works contract unless the purchaser prepares written justification providing reasons why it is in 
the best interest of the Town and its taxpayers to make an award to other than the low bidder. If a 
bidder is not deemed responsible, facts supporting that judgment shall also be documented and 
filed with the record supporting the procurement. Notwithstanding the above all public works 
projects involving construction contracts, as defined in Guideline 2, shall require a contractor and 
subcontractor(s) who is a party to, or working under, a construction contract, to be a participant in 
good standing of a Class "A" qualified apprenticeship program that is registered with and 
approved by the New York State Department of Labor or U.S. Department of Labor and to have 
apprenticeship agreements, as evidenced by valid certificates of completion which are specifically 
identified as pertaining to the specific trade(s) and/or job titles(s) called for within the construction 
contract and appropriate for the type and scope of work to be performed, as set forth and 
described in the bid specifications. In addition, all contractors and subcontractors must submit to 
the Town, Certificates of Completion showing that they, or their sponsor participate in a Class "A" 
qualified apprenticeship program; a Class "A" qualified apprenticeship program shall mean an 
apprenticeship program that has graduated at least one apprentice from a New York State 
Department of Labor or U.S. Department of Labor approved and registered apprenticeship 
program, in the trade and/or job title called for within the construction contract, within a twenty-
four-month time period immediately preceding the bid date of such project. The contractor and/or 
subcontractor must submit to the Town: i) a letter certifying that it currently operates a Class "A" 
Page 13 of 307
qualified apprenticeship program as a sponsor; ii) a letter from the Class "A" qualified 
apprenticeship program's sponsor certifying the contractor and/or subcontractor are signatory to 
the sponsor and that the program is a Class "A" qualified apprenticeship program as defined 
herein; or iii) if the contractor or subcontractor participates in a recently formed apprenticeship 
program, it may satisfy the requirement of this certification by providing documentation showing 
that the Class "A" qualified apprenticeship program in which it participates: a) has been 
established within the past five years; b) is currently registered with and approved by the U.S. 
Department of Labor or a state apprenticeship agency; and c) provides bona fide apprenticeship 
training to participants and is in compliance with the standards and requirements applicable to 
registered apprenticeship programs under 29 CFR 29, including the requirement under these 
rules to maintain as at least one registered apprentice in accordance with the guidelines of 29 
CFR 29.6(a). It shall be a contractor's and/or subcontractor's responsibility to submit the required 
certificates of completion as part of any bid submitted in connection with a construction contract. 
The failure of the contractor and/or subcontractor to provide the required certificates/certifications 
shall disqualify the contractor being awarded, or performing the construction contract or from the 
subcontractor being approved as a subcontractor on the construction contract. Note that it shall 
be the responsibility of the department administering a construction contract to verify that a 
contractor or subcontractor meets these requirements and has included the required 
apprenticeship documentation in his/her bid. 
 
F. Guideline 6. A request for proposal and/or quotation shall not be required under the following 
circumstances: 
 
(1) Contract for professional services subject to resolution of the Town Board. 
 
(2) Purchases. 
 
(a)  Purchase of goods, equipment or supplies or contract for services through qualifying 
contracts let by other New York State political subdivisions provided that: i) the contract must 
have been let by the United States or any agency thereof, any state or any other political 
subdivision or district therein; ii) the contract must have been made policy available for use by 
other governmental entities, i.e., inclusion in the contract let by the other entity of a clause 
extending the terms and conditions of the contract to other governmental entities; and iii) the 
contract must have been let to the lowest responsible bidder or on the basis of best value in a 
manner consistent with General Municipal Law § 103 and related case law. This provision is 
intended to include all future statutory amendments of General Municipal Law § 103, 
Subdivision 16 and same are deemed to be incorporated by reference, as if recited in its 
entirety herein. (*Note that General Municipal Law § 103, Subdivision 16, was originally 
scheduled to expire on August 1, 2017, and has been extended to June 30, 2026). 
 
(b)  Purchase of goods, equipment or supplies or contract for services through qualifying 
contracts let by the County of Suffolk or through any county within the State of New York 
subject to County Law § 408-a. 
 
(3)  Public emergency arising out of an accident or unforeseen occurrence or condition affecting 
public buildings, public property or life, health, safety or property of the inhabitants of the Town. 
Unless exigent circumstances exist such that time is of the essence, purchaser shall seek to 
obtain approval of the Town Board prior to purchase or contract. 
 
(4)  Sole-source situations (purchaser must obtain written verification from vendor). 
 
(5)  Surplus and secondhand supplies, material or equipment from federal government, State of New 
York, or other political subdivisions, districts, or public benefit corporation. 
Page 14 of 307
 
(6)  Purchase of goods, equipment and supplies under state contracts through the Office of General 
Services and subject to rules promulgated by the Office of General Services. 
 
G.  Guideline 7. The purchase of goods or services must be accompanied by a duly signed and 
completed purchase order or contract except a purchase order shall not be required prior to 
purchase/procurement under those circumstances identified in Guideline 6 and purchases or 
public works project less than $1,000. The above guideline is not intended to eliminate the 
requirement for a purchase order. Note, all department heads shall be responsible to verify the 
existence of a bid or contract award for the same or similar goods or services and shall be 
limited to purchase from such vendor/contractor. 
 
H.  Guideline 8. The Town Department responsible for personal property and/or asset(s) it wishes to 
have declared surplus shall complete a "Plant Asset/Office Equipment Disposal Form" including 
a description of the item, serial/tag #, location, condition, estimated surplus value, and reason for 
disposal. The Plant Asset/Office Equipment Disposal Form must be signed by the 
supervisor/manager of the department and filed with the Office of the Financial Administrator. 
Town departments may transfer assets and equipment provided that a Plant Asset/Office 
Equipment Disposal Form records the transfer and same is filed with the Financial Administrator. 
Finally, the Office of the Financial Administrator, with the assistance of department heads, shall 
evaluate estimated surplus value of all assets and equipment disposed on a biannual basis and 
policy make recommendation to the Town Board regarding disposal or sale, subject to all 
applicable laws and procedures regarding disposition of surplus asset/equipment, including but 
not limited to bid procedures set forth in General Municipal Law § 103, and receipt of an "as-is" 
statement and/or liability waiver. Note, all Town logos or other markings identifying the property 
as Town property shall be removed prior to sale. This policy shall not in any way limit or prohibit 
the Town Board from donating an item of personal property and/or asset as permitted under the 
law. For purposes of Guideline 8: "surplus" shall be defined as any Town personal property 
and/or asset that is no longer needed now or in the foreseeable future or that is no longer of 
value or use to the Town and "estimated surplus value" shall be defined as the estimated 
amount of money an interested party will be willing to pay the Town for the property. This can be 
determined through an estimate, an appraisal, Kelly Blue Book value or other sources available 
to the responsible Department or Office of the Financial Administrator. 
 
I.   Guideline 9. This policy shall be reviewed annually by the Town. 
 
J.  Guideline 10. For all contract procurement actions in which the Town of Riverhead is a recipient 
or sub-recipient of a Federal award, compliance with 2 CFR 200.318-327 is mandatory.

9/15/2026 IX.1.  
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-841 
 
ADOPTS SEWER DISTRICT CAPITAL PROJECT #82619 - NYS SECURE GRANT INFRASTRUCTURE 
IMPROVEMENTS 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead (the “Town”) recognizes the importance of 
maintaining and improving essential sewer infrastructure for the health, safety, and welfare of 
the Town’s residents and businesses; and  
 
WHEREAS, under Town Board Resolution 2026-462, adopted on May 5, 2026, The 
Town Board authorized the Community Development Department to apply for grants funded by 
the New York State Environmental Facilities Corporation (“EFC”), to address certain sewer 
department infrastructure needs; and  
 
WHEREAS, the New York State EFC has awarded the Town of Riverhead a 
Strengthening Essential Cybersecurity for Utilities and Resiliency Enhancement (“SECURE”) 
grant, with no match required, in the amount of $278,285.00, to be used for infrastructure and 
engineering costs. Now, therefore, be it,  
 
RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby authorized to establish the following 
budget adjustments; 
 
           FROM                    TO 
  
ES2-3901-000-82619-8 Sewer – NYS SECURE Grant      $ 278,285.00 
 
ES2-8-8130-250-000-82619 Sewer  – Infrastructure                     $ 229,775.00 
 
ES2-8-8130-435-000-82619 Sewer – Prof Svcs - Engineering      $  48,510.00 
 
 
And be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this resolution 
to finance@townofriverheadny.gov, the Community Development Department, the I.T. 
Department and the Sewer District, and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-842 
 
AMBULANCE DISTRICT BUDGET ADJUSTMENT FOR REPAIRS TO THE ARMORY BUILDING 
 
Councilman Kern offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Merrifield 
 
          WHEREAS, Town Board Resolution #2025-423, adopted on May 21, 2025, established a 
capital project for the construction of a new building for the Ambulance District at 1121 Osborn 
Avenue, Riverhead (SCTM# 0600-108.00-2.00-005.000) operated by the Riverhead Volunteer 
Ambulance Corp Inc. (“RVAC”).; and  
 
WHEREAS, RVAC will be required to temporarily relocate to the Armory Building in 
Riverhead, to continue operations during the construction of the new building; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Armory Building is in need of various repairs to enable RVAC to operate 
safely and efficiently during this period; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Supervisor has requested to transfer $45,000 from the Ambulance 
District Fund balance in order to pay for the repairs to the Armory Building; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby 
authorized to establish the following budget adjustments; 
  
                                                                                          FROM                 TO 
 
SM1-9999-000-00000-0 Ambulance District Fund Balance      $45,000.00 
 
SM1-4-4540-402-000-00000 Amb – R&M – Buildings                            $45,000.00 
 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a 
copy of this resolution to finance@townofriverheadny.gov, the Engineering Department and the 
Ambulance District; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy 
of this resolution from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may 
be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-843 
 
BUDGET ADOPTION FOR CAPITAL PROJECT #12620 RESTORATION OF WADING RIVER DUCK 
PONDS 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
          WHEREAS, the Wading River Pond lies within the jurisdictional boundaries of the Town 
of Brookhaven and the Town of Riverhead, and said Pond is in need of restoration work, 
including, but not limited to, dredging, reconstruction and/or replacement of bulkheads and 
landscaping; and 
  
WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead and Town of Brookhaven in recognition of the 
mutually beneficial common goal of working together cooperatively, entered into an 
Intermunicipal Agreement on February 5, 2026, for the planning, development and construction 
of the Wading River Duck Ponds Improvement Project; and  
 
WHEREAS, the terms of the Intermunicipal Agreement allow for costs related to 
engineering work for this capital project to be shared equally (50/50) between the Town of 
Riverhead and the Town of Brookhaven; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Supervisor has requested funding of $40,000 from the General Fund’s 
fund balance to cover the Town of Riverhead’s portion of engineering costs associated with this 
capital project. Now, therefore, be it  
 
RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby authorized to establish the following 
budget adjustments: 
 
                                                                                       FROM                  TO 
 
A01-9999-000-00000-0 Appropriated Fund Balance          $40,000.00 
 
 
    H01-1-1940-435-000-12620 Professional Services - Engineering                $40,000.00 
 
And be it further 
  
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this resolution 
to finance@townofriverheadny.gov, the Engineering Department, the Purchasing Department 
and the Office of the Town Attorney; and be it further 
  
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

MOVER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield

internship through April 1, 2027; and

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-847 
 
APPOINTS AN AUTOMOTIVE EQUIPMENT OPERATOR TO THE HIGHWAY DEPARTMENT 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 64(1), the Town Board has general management 
and control of the finances of the Town; and 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 64(3), the Town Board has general management 
and control of Town property; and  
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 20 and except as otherwise provided by law, the 
Town Board has the responsibility to employ such persons as the Town Board deems 
necessary for the proper conduct of the affairs of the Town; and 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 27, the Town Board shall fix, from time to time, the 
salaries of all officers, officials and employees of said town, whether elected or appointed, and 
determine when the same shall be payable; and  
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Highway Law § 140(4), the Highway Superintendent shall 
employ such persons as may be necessary for the maintenance and repair of Town highways 
and removal of obstructions caused by snow and provide for the supervision over such 
employees, subject to the provisions of law recited above and, including but not limited to, 
available appropriations for such purpose, any applicable civil service requirements; and  
          WHEREAS, the Highway Superintendent filed a Personnel Report and Request for Town 
Board Resolution to Effectuate Personnel Request, to wit appointment of Peter Guido Jr. to the 
position of Automotive Equipment Operator effective September 30, 2026; and 
          WHEREAS, the Highway Superintendent’s report stated that said reappointment is 
necessary for the repair and maintenance of Town highways; and 
          WHEREAS, this position was duly posted in accordance with the terms of the CSEA 
contract, Job Posting #10 of 2026.  
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board accepts the Highway 
Superintendent’s Personnel Report and Request Town Board Resolution to Effectuate 
Personnel Request, to wit: the appointment of Peter Guido Jr. effective September 30, 2026 to 
the position of Automotive Equipment Operator and sets his salary at Group 6, Step 4A of the 
CSEA Operational and Technical Salary Schedule which is $57,912.70 per annum; and be it 
further, 
          RESOLVED, that the Personnel Officer and Financial Administrator are hereby directed 
to take such action to effectuate this personnel matter on behalf of the Highway Superintendent; 
and be it further,   
Page 33 of 307
          RESOLVED that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this resolution to 
the Town Highway Superintendent, Personnel Officer, Financial Administrator, Payroll and 
Town Attorney’s Office; and be it further,   
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Joann Waski 
AYES: None 
NAYS: None

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-854 
 
AUTHORIZING AN AWARD OF CONTRACT TO M&J ENGINEERING, D.P.C. ENGINEERING 
ASSESSMENT FOR THE RESTORATION OF THE WADING RIVER DUCK PONDS, WADING RIVER, NY 
 
Councilman Kern offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Merrifield 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution 2026-152, the Town of Riverhead entered into an 
intermunicipal agreement with the Town of Brookhaven providing for collaboration in furtherance 
of restoring of the Wading River Duck Ponds; and 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to the intermunicipal agreement, the Town of Brookhaven 
Purchasing Division, on behalf of the Town’s Department of Highways, Division of Stormwater, 
advertised Request for Proposals for qualified firms to provide prepare an Engineering 
Assessment for the Restoration of the Wading River Duck Ponds, Wading River, NY (Town of 
Brookhaven RFP No. 26-05); and 
          WHEREAS, copies of the submissions were sent to the Evaluation Committee 
established by the intermunicipal agreement for review and after due deliberation it was 
determined that the proposal submitted by M&J Engineering, D.P.C. best met the needs of the 
Town of Riverhead and the Town of Brookhaven to provide engineering services for the 
assessment of the Wading River Duck Ponds, located in the hamlet of Wading River, NY; and 
 
          WHEREAS, the proposal submitted by M&J Engineering, D.P.C. states a cost to provide 
Engineering Assessment for the Restoration of the Wading River Duck Ponds, Wading River, 
NY not to exceed $78,856.00, and as per the intermunicipal agreement, the Town of Riverhead 
will reimburse the Town of Brookhaven 50 percent of the associated costs, which shall be paid 
based upon approved benchmarks (cost proposal) and an agreed upon payment schedule. 
 
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead, in 
partnership with the Town of Brookhaven, hereby award a contract to M&J Engineering, D.P.C., 
110 Bi-County Boulevard, Suite 101, Farmingdale, NY 11735, to prepare an Engineering 
Assessment for the Restoration of the Wading River Duck Ponds, Wading River, NY in an 
amount not to exceed $78,856.00, and per the intermunicipal agreement, the Town of 
Riverhead will reimburse the Town of Brookhaven 50 percent of the cost associated therewith; 
and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk be and is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this 
resolution to the Engineering Department, Accounting Department and Office of the Town 
Attorney; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilman Robert Kern 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-855 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD RESOLUTION 2026- ORDER ESTABLISHING EXTENSION TO RIVERHEAD 
SEWER DISTRICT TO SERVE 840 OLD COUNTRY ROAD, RIVERHEAD (SCTM# 0600-82-03-9.1)  
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
          WHEREAS, a petition was filed by Peter Moutafis, the owner of parcel located at 840 Old 
Country Road, Riverhead, New York, requesting that the proposed retail cannabis dispensary 
development at the site (SCTM No. 0600-82-03-9.1) be served by the Riverhead Sewer District; 
and 
 
          WHEREAS, the proposed development is not located within the existing boundaries of 
the Riverhead Sewer District; and 
 
          WHEREAS, a map and plan dated April 8, 2025 have been prepared by H2M, consulting 
engineers to the Riverhead Sewer District, detailing the necessary measures and costs 
associated with construction required to the lateral sewer mains for the proposed development; 
and 
 
          WHEREAS, the project will include the construction of (1) 6,060 square foot metal 
building along the north side of the existing 8,368 square foot masonry building. The proposed 
buildout of the subject site will result in a total combined floor area of 14,428 square feet, with an 
estimated wastewater flow of 536 gallons per day; and           
 
          WHEREAS, the map and plan were available for review and inspection at the Office of 
the Riverhead Town Clerk, 4 West Main Street, Riverhead, New York, during normal business 
hours; and  
 
          WHEREAS, all costs associated with this extension and all required denitrification fees 
based on flows as calculated by the Riverhead Sewer District shall be borne by the owners of 
the property which is the subject of this extension; and  
 
          WHEREAS, it has been determined that this is an “unlisted action” under the State 
Environmental Quality Review Act, the implementation of which, as proposed will not result in 
any significant environmental effects; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the Town Board held a public hearing on the 1st day of September, 2026 
regarding this proposed sewer main extension, wherein all persons wishing to be heard were 
heard.  
 
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that upon the proceedings, plans and 
specifications, and the public hearing, the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead, as governing 
body of the Riverhead Sewer District, hereby approves the extension of the boundary of the 
Riverhead Sewer District to accommodate sewer connection to 840 Old Country Road, 
Riverhead (SCTM# 0600-82-03-9.1) within the assessing designation SE/H2M Project NO. 
RDSD 26-01 all subject to the following conditions: 
Page 52 of 307
 
The required denitrification fees based on flows calculated by the Riverhead Sewer District shall 
be posted by Owner/Petitioner to the Town of Riverhead Financial Administrator upon request of 
Owner/Petitioner for service by the Riverhead Sewer District. 
 
The improvements of said extension, on site and off site, design and construction costs 
estimated in the map and plan are at no cost to the District but at sole cost of the 
Owner/Petitioner. Note, in the event Owner/Petitioner delays the project or in the event 
authorization to initiate final approval requires coordination with NYSDEC such that the District 
improvements summarized in the map and plan are delayed 6-months from the date of this Map 
and Plan, the Map and Plan may need to be revised to reflect updated budgets. 
 
To the extent applicable and required for the improvements set forth in the Map & Plan, this 
resolution shall be contingent upon the granting of easement(s) to the extent required for the 
installation and construction and maintenance of the sewer connection, manholes and 
appurtenances and unobstructed surface access fo4r repair and replace of same, all costs of tile 
insurance, survey, mapping and recording to be borne by the Owner/Petitioner; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the terms and conditions of this Order shall be accepted and agreed to 
by the Owner/Petitioner whose consent shall be duly acknowledged and shall be binding on the 
heirs and assigns of the Owner/Petitioner and shall run with the land and shall be effective upon 
filing with the Town Clerk; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is directed to forward a copy of this resolution to 
Owner/Petitioner Peter Moutafis 840 Old Country Road, Riverhead, New York, 11901 and 
Vincent J. Trimarco Sr., Esq. 1040 W Jericho Tpke, Smithtown NY 11787, and record a copy of 
this resolution with the Suffolk County Clerk and file a copy with the Riverhead Board of 
Assessors; and be it further,  
 
          RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
 
AGREED TO AND ACCEPTED BY: PETER MOUTAFIS  
 
By: ______________________________  
Name: Peter Moutafis 
Title: Owner/Petitioner 
Date: 
Sworn to me before me this ___day of _______________, 2026. 
BY ORDER OF THE TOWN BOARD   
AS GOVERNING BODY OF THE RIVERHEAD SEWER DISTRICT  
JAMES WOOTEN, Town Clerk

RATIFIES AUTHORIZATION FOR THE SUPERVISOR TO EXECUTE RENEWAL AND AMENDMENT 
AGREEMENT FOR THE RIVERHEAD CENTRAL SCHOOL DISTRICT SCHOOL RESOURCE OFFICER 
PROGRAM

          WHEREAS, the Riverhead Central School District Board of Education and the Town of

Program with the Riverhead Central School District; and 
 
WHEREAS, the School Board and Town recognize the outstanding benefits of the 
School Resource Officer Program to the citizens of the Town of Riverhead and to the students 
of the Riverhead Central School District; and

Riverhead to continue this program.

and be it further,

Page 57 of 307
RENEWAL AND AMENDMENT AGREEMENT BETWEEN 
THE BOARD OF EDUCATION OF THE RIVERHEAD CENTRAL SCHOOL DISTRICT 
and 
THE TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
for 
THE SCHOOL RESOURCE OFFICER PROGRAM 
 
This agreement is made and entered on the ______ day of September, 2026 between THE BOARD 
OF EDUCATION OF THE RIVERHEAD CENTRAL SCHOOL DISTRICT, RIVERHEAD, NEW 
YORK (hereinafter referred to as the “School Board” or “District” or “School District”), and THE 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD (hereinafter referred to as the “Town”); 
 
WHEREAS, the School Board and the Town have a long history of successfully working together 
with the common goal of providing for the safety and well-being of the District’s children; and 
 
WHEREAS, a School Resource Officer Program has been proposed for the Riverhead Central School 
District as hereinafter described; and 
 
WHEREAS, the School Board and Town recognize the potential outstanding benefits of the School 
Resource Officer Program to the citizens of the Town of Riverhead and particularly to the students 
of the Riverhead Central School District; and 
 
WHEREAS, it is in the best interests of the School Board and the citizens of the Town of Riverhead 
to establish this program. 
 
IT IS THEREFORE AGREED that the Town’s Police Department shall supply two (2) police officers 
to the Riverhead Central School District to be assigned to the schools based on the terms and 
conditions set forth in the parties’ July 29, 2024 School Resource Officer agreement except as to those 
terms as may be specifically amended herein below.  
 
ARTICLE I 
Term of Agreement 
 
A School Resource Officer Program is hereby established in the public school system of the 
Riverhead Central School System, effective for the 2026-2027 school year.  The Agreement shall 
thereafter be negotiated for any subsequent years through collaboration between the Riverhead 
Central School District Board of Education and the Riverhead Town Board in advance of Riverhead 
Central School District’s annual budget preparations. The School Board's administrators will conduct 
a one-day orientation before the School Resource Officers assume duties at the assigned school.  The 
terms and conditions of any future agreements, including but not limited to the provisions contained 
herein, shall be negotiated between the parties and memorialized in writing.   
 
ARTICLE II 
Financing of the School Resource Officer Program 
 
Reimbursement for the SRO  
 
For the School Year commencing September 2, 2027 through June 30, 2027 the Riverhead School 
Board shall pay the Town of Riverhead the total sum of $200,000.00 for the two (2) SROs.  The Town 
shall issue a memorandum and invoice to the District detailing the cost of the SROs on a quarterly 
basis and payment shall be due within forty-five (45) days of mailing.  
Page 58 of 307
 
B. Overtime 
 
1. The School Board will not be responsible for any overtime costs over and above the amount 
set forth at paragraph “A” above, including costs incurred as a result of the SRO's (or his/her 
replacement) participation, at the discretion of the Superintendent or his designee, in any 
extracurricular activities such as athletic events, concerts, hearings, proms, and homecoming dances 
or after school or evening meetings.   
 
2. The Director of Security and Safety or other District administrator will notify the SRO and 
get approval for any overtime details, at least four (4) working days prior to the commencement of 
said event.  If it is deemed by the Director of Security and Safety or District administrator and the 
SRO that the occasion will require additional SRO presence, that presence should be requested and 
approved within that four (4) day time frame.  To facilitate logistics and planning, a minimum of four 
(4) days’ notice of the need of overtime shall be given if possible.  There is no guarantee that a SRO 
will be provided if less than four (4) days’ notice is given. 
 
ARTICLE III 
Appointment of School Resource Officers 
 
A. The Police Department shall have the sole discretion in selecting the two School Resource 
Officers for the term of this agreement.  Police Officer Eric Cohen will be assigned primarily to the 
Riverhead High School and Police Officer Kaley Castantine will be assigned primarily to the Middle 
School. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have caused this Agreement to be signed by their duly 
authorized officers on the date written above.

AUTHORIZES SUPERVISOR TO EXECUTE AN AGREEMENT WITH NYS ENVIRONMENTAL FACILITIES 
CORPORATION (NYSEFC) 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, the United States of America, pursuant to the Federal Act, requires each 
state to establish a revolving fund to be administered by an instrumentality of the state as a 
condition to its receipt of capitalization grants under the Federal Act; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the State pursuant to the State Act has established the Revolving Fund to be 
used for purposes of satisfying this requirement of the Federal Act; and   
   
          WHEREAS, the NYS Environmental Facilities Corporation (NYSEFC) has the 
responsibility, together with the Agency, to administer the Revolving Fund and to provide 
financial assistance from the Revolving Fund to eligible recipients for the construction of Eligible 
Projects, as provided in the State Act and the Federal Act; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead has submitted to the NYSEFC an application for 
financial assistance from the Revolving Fund for the purpose of financing or refinancing the 
planning, design, acquisition, construction and installation of the Class A Biosolids Upgrade 
Project for Riverhead Sewer District (“Project”), and the NYSEFC has reviewed and approved 
the financing of the Project; and  
 
          WHEREAS, on the basis of such application and the representations, warranties and 
covenants set forth herein, the NYSEFC proposes to make funding available to the Town, 
pursuant to Article IV, to (i) finance, or to reimburse the Town for costs incurred in connection 
with the Project, (ii) prefund any Third-Party Funding, if applicable, or (iii) refinance Existing 
Indebtedness, if applicable; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the Town desires to receive the proceeds of the Financing upon the terms 
and conditions set forth in this Project Finance Agreement, the State Act and the NYSEFC Act; 
and  
 
          WHEREAS, if the Town has received, or will receive, a commitment from a Third-Party 
Funding Entity for Third-Party Funding, that amount, together with other funds available or 
expected to be available to the Recipient, is expected to be sufficient to pay all costs of the 
Project; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the Financing made by the NYSEFC pursuant to this Project Finance 
Agreement shall be accomplished by the issuance by the Town and purchase by the NYSEFC 
of one or more Recipient Financing Instruments, which shall be subject to mandatory 
redemption from and to the extent of amounts received by the Town as the proceeds of the 
Third-Party Funding on or after the date of the Town Financing Instruments; and  
 
          WHEREAS, any required approval of engineering or facilities plans or reports with respect 
Page 60 of 307
to the Project has been obtained by the Town, subject to the provisions of applicable State 
environmental standards set forth in law, rules and regulations. NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT 
RESOLVED that the Town Board, be and hereby, authorizes the Supervisor to execute an 
Agreement with NYS Environmental Facilities Corporation (NYSEFC), in substantially the form 
attached, for a Class A Biosolids Upgrade Project; and be it further, 
           
          RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this resolution to 
the Sewer District, Office of the Town Attorney and the Accounting Department; and be it 
further,  
 
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a copy of same may be obtained 
from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
 
THE VOTE

MOVER: Councilman Kenneth Rothwell 
SECONDER: Councilman Robert Kern

Article I. GENERAL PROVISIONS 
 
 Definitions. Unless stated otherwise, each capitalized term used in this Project 
Finance Agreement has the meaning specified for it in EXHIBIT D. 
 
 Effective Date and Term . This Project Finance Agreement is effective and shall 
become enforceable as of the date on the cover page hereof following its execution and delivery by the 
Recipient and the NYSEFC and shall remain in full force and effect until Full Repayment; provided, 
however that Article V and Article VI shall survive any termination of this Project Finance Agreement.  
Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, in the event that there has been Full Repayment but there 
is still a Grant that has not been fully disbursed to the Recipient, this Project Finance Agreement shall 
remain in effect until September 17, 2031, provided, however that Article V and Article VI shall survive 
any termination of this Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 Approvals and Consents. All approvals, consents, determinations and acceptances 
required to be given or made by any person or party hereunder shall be at the sole discretion of the person 
or party whose approval, consent, determination or acceptance is required. 
 
 Interpretation. The captions, headings and table of contents are solely for 
convenience of reference and shall not constitute part of this Project Finance Agreement. They do not affect its 
meaning, construction or effect. The parties acknowledge and agree that this Project Finance Agreement  
shall not be construed more favorably in favor of any party hereto based upon which party drafted the same. 
 
 Exhibits and Appendices Incorporated . All exhibits and appendices to this Project 
Finance Agreement, including any amendments and supplements hereto, are hereby incorporated in and made 
a part of this Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 Amendments. Any provision of this Project Finance Agreement may be amended, 
but only if, such amendment is in writing and is signed by the Recipient and the NYSEFC. 
 
 Assignment. The rights of the NYSEFC to enforce the duties, covenants, obligations 
and agreements of the Recipient set forth in this Project Finance Agreement may at any time, in whole or in 
part, be assigned and pledged by the NYSEFC. Thereafter, such duties, covenants, obligations and agreements 
assigned and pledged shall be for the benefit of and enforceable by the NYSEFC and such assignee. The 
Recipient may not transfer or assign this Project Finance Agreement without the prior written consent of the 
NYSEFC. Any purported transfer in violation of this Section 1.07 shall be null and void. The provisions of 
this Project Finance Agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the parties hereto and their 
respective successors and assigns. 
 
 Applicable Law; Venue. This Project Finance Agreement and the rights and duties 
of the parties, shall be construed and determined in accordance with the laws of the State without regard to 
conflicts of law principles that would require application of the laws of another jurisdiction. Each party

Article III. AGREEMENT TO PROVIDE FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE 
  
 Agreement to Provide Financial Assistance for Project Costs  from the Revolving 
Fund. 
 
The NYSEFC shall provide Financing to the Recipient pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement, 
solely from the sources and to the extent specified in Section 3.02, in an amount not greater than the Maximum 
Principal Sum set forth in EXHIBIT E (as it may be amended in accordance with the terms hereof) as the 
amount requested or available for Financing. The Financing proceeds will be disbursed to the Recipient in 
accordance with the terms of Article IV and the Recipient Financing Instruments. In no event shall (i) the 
Principal Amount exceed the total Project Costs  or (ii) the aggregate Maximum Principal Sum of this 
Financing, together with any other indebtedness of the Recipient issued to finance the Project or to refinance 
Existing Indebtedness for the Project, exceed the amount of debt authorized by the Recipient, as of the time 
this Financing is made. 
 
If the Recipient receives funds from any Third-Party Funding Entity for Project Costs that are not 
being pre-financed under this Project Finance  Agreement, the Recipient  must expend such funds before 
requesting any disbursement of Financing proceeds under this Project Finance Agreement.  
 
In addition to the foregoing, s ubject to the conditions and in accordance with the terms of this 
Project Finance Agreement, the NYSEFC will provide financial assistance to the Recipient by making the 
Grant available to the Recipient. The NYSEFC shall have no obligation to make payments of the Grant 
more frequently than once every month. The NYSEFC will retain custody and control over Grant funds 
which will only be made available to the Recipient upon submission to the NYSEFC of documentation of 
incurred Project Costs and approval thereof by the NYSEFC. The Recipient further hereby agrees that the 
NYSEFC, in its sole discretion, may make all or a portion of each Disbursement hereunder from proceeds 
of the Grant and/or from proceeds of the Financing. 
 
 Sources of Funding; Nature of Obligations.  
 
 Sources of Funding.   The NYSEFC shall have no obligation to make any 
Disbursement pursuant to Section 4.02 , except from the sources provided for in this Section 3.02 . 
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Project Finance Agreement, the Recipient acknowledges 
and agrees that the NYSEFC’s funding of any Disbursement of the Financing, as provided in Section 4.02 
of this Project Finance Agreement, is subject to the receipt by the NYSEFC of monies in an amount equal 
to the aggregate principal amount of such Disbursement from monies made available to the NYSEFC as 
capitalization grant payments under the Federal Act (and such amount is allocated by the NYSEFC to the 
Financing) or from moneys made available to the NYSEFC for purposes of the Grant, unless the NYSEFC 
determines to provide other available monies for such purpose. The NYSEFC has no obligation to make 
any Disbursements, and no obligation shall be incurred by the State or the NYSEFC, beyond moneys made 
available to the NYSEFC for such purposes.

the time of payment shall be legal tender for the payment of public and private debts, and that each payment

 Financing Clauses. 
 
(a) Disbursements. Subject to the conditions and in accordance with the terms of this 
Project Finance Agreement (including but not limited to Section 4.04 ), the NYSEFC hereby agrees to 
provide the Financing by making disbursements of funds (“Disbursements”) to the Recipient from time to 
time, during the period from the date set forth on the cover page to and including the date each such 
Disbursement is due by reason of maturity or earlier redemption of the Recipient Financing Instruments, in 
an aggregate amount not to exceed the Maximum Amount specified in EXHIBIT E. Disbursements may 
include amounts drawn under the Recipient Financing Instruments or disbursed under the Grant . The 
NYSEFC shall have no obligation to make Disbursements more frequently than once a week, and the 
NYSEFC shall not be required to honor any request for a Disbursement received by it on or after the tenth 
Page 77 of 307
 
11 
 
Business Day preceding the date of maturity or earlier redemption of the related Recipient Financing 
Instruments. 
 
The Recipient shall be obligated to repay each Disbursement made pursuant to the Recipient 
Financing Instruments in one or more principal installments in the amount(s) and at the time(s) specified in, or 
determined in accordance with, EXHIBIT E. No interest shall accrue on Disbursements prior to the date on 
which repayment of such amount is due by reason of maturity or earlier redemption of the Recipient Financing 
Instruments. The Recipient shall pay interest at the Overdue Interest Rate on the Principal Amount on and after 
any date on which such Principal Amount was due. 
  
The Recipient agrees that the NYSEFC, in its sole discretion, may allocate all or a portion 
of each Disbursement to the Grant or to the Maximum Principal Sum of the Recipient Financing Instruments, 
and may allocate any amounts drawn under this Financing among the Project Costs. The NYSEFC at any 
time may seek to recoup Grant proceeds from the Recipient if the NYSEFC determines that the Recipient was 
overpaid Grant proceeds. In addition, notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary, the 
NYSEFC shall not be required to honor any request for a Disbursement received by it after five (5) years 
from date of the Project Completion Certificate or five (5) years after the Closing Date, whichever is later. 
Failure of Recipient to complete the Project within five years after date of the Project Completion Certificate 
or five (5) years after the Closing Date, whichever is later, may result in Recipient’s forfeiture of undisbursed 
Financing proceeds, if any, upon notice by the NYSEFC to the Recipient. 
 
(b) Recording of Disbursements and Payments. All Disbursements, interest rates, and 
payments made on account of the unpaid principal balance of a Recipient Financing Instrument shall be 
recorded by or on behalf of the NYSEFC and endorsed on the grid or grids attached to such Recipient 
Financing Instrument, which record shall be conclusive ; provided, however, that such recording and 
endorsement of Disbursements and payments shall be made and shall be effective only upon receipt of and 
in accordance with the terms of a Supplemental Certificate in substantially the form of EXHIBIT L and 
executed by the Chief Fiscal Officer of the Recipient . The “Maximum Principal Amount” listed on a 
particular grid shall be the maximum amount of Disbursements that the Recipient may draw for a given 
project number at the interest rate provided on such grid.  
 
(c) Principal Repayment after Construction Completion. The Recipient agrees it shall 
commence principal payments in compliance with the Local Finance Law but no later than one year after 
the completion of construction of the Project. 
 
 Redemption Provisions. 
 
 Mandatory Redemption . The Recipient Financing Instruments then outstanding 
shall be redeemed by the Recipient at any time, in whole or in part, by any method of redemption which the 
NYSEFC, in its sole discretion, deems necessary or desirable, at the Redemption Price, in accordance with 
the terms thereof, the provisions of the next succeeding paragraphs and, if applicable, EXHIBIT E. On or 
prior to the date of redemption, the Recipient shall pay to the NYSEFC the Redemption Price of the 
Recipient Financing Instruments being redeemed, together with all other sums due and owing to the 
NYSEFC hereunder. The Recipient Financing Instruments shall be redeemed by the Recipient, in whole, 
prior to maturity, pursuant to and in accordance with this Section 4.03(a) if, following the award of the last 
major contract relating to the Project, (i) Recipient fails to satisfy all conditions precedent to the conversion 
of this Financing to Long-Term Financing within 60 days of receipt of a written notice from the NYSEFC 
setting forth such conditions precedent, (ii) Long-Term Financing is made available to the Recipient by the 
NYSEFC, and (iii) the NYSEFC makes written demand of the Recipient for such redemption.

12 
 
The Recipient Financing Instruments shall be redeemed by the Recipient, in whole or in part, 
prior to maturity pursuant to and in accordance with this Section 4.03(a) upon the date specified in a notice 
from the NYSEFC delivered to the Recipient not less than sixty (60) days prior to such redemption date upon 
the occurrence, as determined by the NYSEFC and as specified in such notice, that the Financed Project related 
to the Recipient Financing Instruments is abandoned by the Recipient or in accordance with the terms and 
conditions set forth in Section 6.02 hereof. 
 
The Recipient Financing Instruments shall be redeemed by the Recipient, in whole or in part, 
prior to maturity, within five (5) Business Days after the date on which the Recipient receives any proceeds of 
any Third-Party Funding which is intended to pay any costs of the Project which have been financed with the 
Financing made available pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement; provided, however, that the Recipient 
Financing Instruments shall be redeemed solely to the extent of, and in an amount equal to, the proceeds of 
such Third-Party Funding received by the Recipient on or after the dated date of the Recipient Financing 
Instruments and the Recipient Financing Instruments shall be permanently reduced in an amount equal to the 
proceeds of such Third-Party Funding. If such Third-Party Funding is not paid to the NYSEFC within five (5) 
Business Days after the receipt of the Third-Party Funding, the Recipient Financing Instruments or portions 
thereof in the sum of said Third-Party Funding shall bear interest until paid at the Overdue Interest Rate 
effective five (5) Business Days after the receipt of the Third-Party Funding and continue until such Third-
Party Funding is paid to the NYSEFC. 
 
 Optional Redemption. Unless otherwise provided herein or in EXHIBIT E, the 
Recipient, at any time, may redeem the outstanding Recipient Financing Instruments, in whole at any time, 
or in part on any interest payment date, or, if the Recipient Financing Instruments bear no interest, on any date, 
by any method of redemption acceptable to the NYSEFC, at the Redemption Price, upon compliance with 
the following conditions: 
 
(i) The Recipient shall deliver a written notice to the NYSEFC, at least thirty 
(30) days and not more than sixty (60) days prior to the date of redemption, indicating that the Recipient desires 
to redeem all or a specified amount of the Recipient Financing Instruments outstanding, and specifying the date 
of redemption; and 
 
(ii) On or prior to the date of redemption, the Recipient shall pay to the NYSEFC 
the Redemption Price of the Recipient Financing Instruments being redeemed, together with all other sums due 
and owing to the NYSEFC hereunder. 
 
 Application of Payments. Unless the Recipient and the NYSEFC otherwise agree, 
any redemption payment made pursuant to this Section shall be applied to the redemption of Disbursements 
in the order in which they were made, and the NYSEFC shall note such redemption on the grid attached to 
the appropriate Recipient Financing Instruments. Unless the NYSEFC otherwise agrees, not more than one 
partial redemption of a Recipient Financing Instruments shall be made per month. 
 
 Application of Excess Proceeds. Intentionally Omitted.

Article V. COVENANTS 
 
 Payment; Compliance with Laws and Agreements. 
 
 Project Compliance. The Recipient shall comply, and it shall require its authorized 
representatives, contractors, subcontractors , and consultants paid with funds provided pursuant to this 
Project Finance Agreement to comply at all times with all applicable federal, State, and local laws, statutes, 
regulations, ordinances, rules, and Executive Orders applicable to it and them (including, without limitation if 
applicable, the Davis-Bacon Act, Executive Order 11246, Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, Section 504 
of the Rehabilitation Act of 1973, the Age Discrimination Act of 1975, Federal Funding Accountability and 
Transparency Act, Section 608 of the Clean Water Act, Title IX of the Education Amendments of 1972, Section 
13 of the Federal Water Pollution Control Act Amendments of 1972, 2 CFR 200.300, and Executive Order 
13798). The Recipient shall ensure that the Project will effectively protect water quality, employ good 
management practices and fulfill all federal and State requirements, all requirements of this Project Finance 
Agreement, and all applicable instructions issued by the Commissioner to ensure that these requirements are 
met. The Recipient shall comply with Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964. This includes, among other 
things, designating a civil rights coordinator, establishing a civil rights non -discrimination program , 
providing notices of non-discrimination, maintaining compliance information, and requiring all contractors 
and subcontractors to have instituted grievance procedures to assure the prompt and fair resolution of 
complaints when a violation of Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 or Title 40 CFR Part 5 or 7 is 
alleged. Upon request by the NYSEFC, the Recipient shall promptly provide the NYSEFC, with evidence 
of its, and its authorized representatives, contractors, subcontractors and consultants paid with funds 
provided pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement, compliance with all applicable federal, State, and local 
laws, statutes, regulations, ordinances, rules, and Executive Orders applicable to it and them. If the Project is 
determined by the NYSEFC to be funded using funds directly made available by a State Revolving Fund 
capitalization grant, the Recipient shall comply with the requirements of the Single Audit Act of 1984 (31 
U.S.C. 7501 et seq.) and all laws and regulations implementing same, including without limitation 2 CFR Part 
200 Subpart F, all as may be amended from time to time (collectively “SAA”). This shall include, without 
Page 82 of 307
 
16 
 
limitation, (i) conducting an SAA audit if more than $750,000 in any type or combination of Federal financial 
assistance is received by the Recipient in a fiscal year; (ii) if requested by the NYSEFC, promptly providing 
the NYSEFC with a copy of the SAA audit if more than $750,000 in any type or combination of Federal 
financial assistance is received by the Recipient in a fiscal year ; and (iii) informing the NYSEFC of any 
findings, recommendations, and corrective action plans pertaining to the Revolving Fund contained in any SAA 
audits so that the NYSEFC  can adequately review, approve, and monitor the Recipient . If the Project is 
determined by the NYSEFC to be funded using funds directly made available by a State Revolving Fund 
capitalization grant, the Recipient shall comply with the requirements of 40 U.S.C. 1101 et seq. regarding the 
procurement of architectural and engineering services. 
 
 Enforcement. Regardless of acceptance by the Agency or the NYSEFC  of a 
certification by the Recipient that a Project requirement has been met, the Recipient shall permit the Agency 
or the NYSEFC to take any actions necessary to confirm the accuracy of such certification. The making of 
payments by the NYSEFC does not constitute an acknowledgment or agreement by the NYSEFC that the 
Recipient is in compliance with the terms and conditions of this Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 Business Participation Opportunities for New York State Certified Minority - And

and Women. The Recipient acknowledges that contracts for the Project that are paid for with funds provided

140-145 (the “MWBE Regulations”) and, for such purposes, any such contract shall be considered a State 
Contract as defined the rein. The Recipient shall, to the fullest extent permitted by law , comply with the

Pollution Control Act for contracts for the Project that are paid f or with funds provided pursuant to this 
Project Finance Agreement. The Recipient shall, to the fullest extent permitted by law , comply with Title 
VII of the Civil Rights Act of 1964, 41 CFR Part 60 -1 Subpart A, and 41 CFR Part 60 -4 for construction 
contracts for the Project that are paid for with funds provided pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement. 
The Recipient shall, to the fullest extent permitted by law, require all contractors and subcontractors to have 
instituted grievance procedures to assure the prompt and fair resolution of complaints when a violation of 
Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 or Title 40 CFR Part 5 or 7 is alleged. Recipient  acknowledges, 
and agrees, to the fullest extent permitted by law,  to advise all contractors, subcontractors and consultants 
with contracts for the project that are paid for with funds provided pursuant to this Project Finance 
Agreement, that the MWBE goals in effect at the time of execution of each contract shall be applied to this 
Financing. The Recipient  shall, t o the fullest extent permitted by law , provide to the NYSEFC  such 
documentation as it receives from contractors, subcontractors and consultants, including quarterly periodic 
reports, as may be required by law or requested by the NYSEFC  regarding affirmative action, equal 
employment opportunity (“EEO”), and minority- and women-owned business enterprises (“MWBE”). The 
Recipient shall, to the fullest extent permitted by law, require all contractors, subcontractors and consultants 
to submit no later than execution of contracts: (i) an MWBE Utilization Plan (prime contractors only); (ii) 
an EEO policy statement; and (iii) an acceptable EEO staffing plan for non -construction contracts. Upon 
receipt, the Recipient shall, to the fullest extent permitted by law , submit to the NYSEFC all reports and 
documentation required pursuant to this paragraph.  To the fullest extent permitted by law  the Recipient’s 
approval of a Utilization Plan or waiver request is subject to the prior consent of the NYSEFC. To the fullest 
extent permitted by law  the Recipient shall document and submit to the NYSEFC information received

 Business Participation Opportunities for New York State Certified Service -
Disabled Veteran-Owned Businesses (“SDVOB”). New York State Veterans’ Service Law Article 3 and 9 
NYCRR Part 252 provide for more meaningful participation in public procurement by SDVOBs, thereby 
further integrating such businesses into New York State’s economy. The NYSEFC recognizes the need to 
promote the employment of service-disabled veterans and to ensure that certified service-disabled veteran-
owned businesses have opportunities for maximum feasible participation in the performance of the 
NYSEFC’s contracts. In recognition of the service and sacrifices made by service-disabled veterans and in 
recognition of thei r economic activity in doing business in New York State, the Recipient  is strongly 
encouraged and expected to consider SDVOBs in the fulfillment of the requirements of this Project Finance 
Agreement. Such participation may be as subcontractors or suppliers, as protégés, or in other partnering or 
supporting roles. The Recipient  is encouraged to make good faith efforts to promote and assist in the 
participation of SDVOBs on this Project Finance Agreement for the provision of services and materials. 
The directory of New York State Certified SDVOBs can be viewed at: 
http://ogs.ny.gov/Core/SDVOBA.asp. The Recipient  is encouraged to contact the Office of General 
Services’ Division of Service -Disabled Veteran’s Business Development at 518 -474-2015 or 
VeteransDevelopment@ogs.ny.gov to discuss methods of maximizing participation by SDVOBs on the 
Contract.

 Trafficking in Persons . The Recipient  represents that it will not, nor will its 
employees: (i) engage in severe forms of trafficking in persons, (ii) procure a commercial sex act, or (iii) 
use forced labor in the completion of the Project. For purposes of this Section 5.01(p) the term “employees” 
shall mean either: i) an individual employed by the Recipient who is engaged in services related to the 
Project; or ii) another person engaged in services related to the Project not compensated by Recipient 
including, but not limited to, a volunteer or individual whose services are contributed by a third party as an 
in-kind contribution toward cost sharing or matching requirements.  The term “forced labor” shall mean 
Page 85 of 307
 
19 
 
labor obtained by any of the following methods: the recruitment, harboring, transportation, provision, or 
obtaining of a person for labor or services, through the use of force, fraud, or coercion for the purposes of 
subjection to involuntary servitude, peonage, debt bondage, or slavery.  The terms “severe forms of 
trafficking in persons,” “commercial sex act,” and “coercion” shall have the meanings set forth in Section 
103 of the Trafficking Victims Protection Act (22 USC Chapter 78).

 Tax Covenant. Notwithstanding any other provision hereof, the Recipient shall not 
take or authorize any action or permit any action within its reasonable control to be taken, or fail to take 
any action within its reasonable control, with respect to the Project, or any portion of the proceeds made 
available to it as part of th is financing, including any amounts treated as proceeds of Short -Term Debt 
Obligations for any purpose of Section 103 of the Code, which will result in the loss of the exclusion of 
interest on the Short -Term Debt Obligations from gross income for federal i ncome tax purposes under 
Section 103 of the Code. This provision shall control in case of conflict or ambiguity with any other 
provision of this Project Finance Agreement. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, the Recipient 
shall not take any action or omit to take any action within its reasonable control, which, assuming the 
Recipient Financing Instruments were issued as bonds the interest on which is excluded from gross income 
for federal income tax purposes under Section 103(a) of the Code, would cause the Recipient Financing 
Instruments to be “private activity bonds” or “arbitrage bonds” within the meaning of Sections 141(a) or 
148 of the Code as in effect upon the issuance of the Recipient Financing Instruments or Bonds.  In 
furtherance of such covenants and agreements, the Recipient shall deliver on or prior to the date of the first 
Disbursement under this Project Finance Agreement, if requested by the NYSEFC, an Arbitrage and Use

Recipient shall comply with the provisions thereof, which provisions are incorporated herein as if set forth

fully herein. The Recipient (or any related person, as defined in Section 147(a)(2) of the Code) shall not,

contained in Section 306 of the Clean Air Act and Section 508 of the Clean Water Act, Executive Order 11738, 
and all laws and regulations implementing same; (ix) the Uniform Relocation & Real Property Acquisition 
Policies Act 42 U.S.C. §4601-4655/ P.L. 91-646 and all laws and regulations implementing same; (x) the 
suspension and debarment provisions of Executive Order 125492, CFR Part 180, and 2 CFR Part 1532; (xi) 
the provisions relating to project labor agreements pursuant to Executive Order 13502; (xii) the flood risk 
management provisions of Executive Order 14030; (xi ii) the Build America, Buy America provisions 
contained in the Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act, P.L. 117-58, Secs 70911 – 70927, and all laws and 
regulations implementing same; and (xiv) all cross-cutting federal authorities as required pursuant to 40 CFR 
35.3145. 
 
 Project Signage. During construction of the Project , the Recipient shall post 
signage at the Project site to educate the public about the benefits of the Project. Signage must be placed in 
an easily visible location that can be directly linked to the work taking place and must be maintained in 
good condition throughout the construction of the Project. All Project  signage shall conform to the 
NYSEFC’s then-applicable signage guidance located at https://efc.ny.gov/construction-signs, as may be 
updated and amended from time to time in the NYSEFC’s sole discretion or such other terms as may be 
requested by the NYSEFC  pursuant to the NYSEFC ’s applicable programmatic requirements . The 
Recipient consents to the NYSEFC ’s use of images, descriptions and depictions of the Project for 
promotional and educational presentations, outreach, and publications. 
 
 Required Terms for Project Contracts and Subcontracts . The Recipient  shall 
include in all contracts that are to be paid with funds provided pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement 
the contractual provisions set forth in the NYSEFC ’s then-applicable terms and conditions  located at 
https://efc.ny.gov/terms-conditions, as may be updated and amended from time to time in the NYSEFC’s 
sole discretion  or such other terms as may be requested by the NYSEFC  pursuant to the NYSEFC ’s 
applicable programmatic requirements. The Recipient is strongly encouraged to seek assistance from the 
NYSEFC if it has any questions regarding the applicability of any required contractual language . In 
addition, to the fullest extent feasible , the Recipient  shall avoid infrastructure, building systems or 
equipment that can be used for the combustion of fossil fuels, excluding the necessary use for backup 
Page 87 of 307
 
21 
 
emergency generation and process loads, provided that the Recipient  shall avoid the use of backup 
emergency diesel generators where practicable. 
 
 Plans and Specifications. 
 
 Design and Construction. The Recipient shall provide all plans and specifications 
to the NYSEFC  a minimum of 60 days prior to bidding. The Recipient  shall construct the  Project in 
accordance with plans and specifications delivered to and approved by  the Agency or the NYSEFC and 
consistent with EXHIBIT A. The Recipient shall proceed with the acquisition and expeditious construction 
of the Project in conformity with law, with this Project Finance Agreement and with all applicable 
requirements of governmental authorities having jurisdiction with respect thereto, subject t o such 
modifications of plans and specifications as may be approved by the Agency or the NYSEFC as necessary 
or advisable to effectuate the purposes of the NYSEFC Act and the State Act. If construction of the Project 
is not complete on the Closing Date, t he Recipient shall complete the Project within five years from the 
effective date of this Project Finance Agreement unless the Recipient requests an extension in writing and 
the NYSEFC approves in writing such extension. 
 
 Project Completion Certificate. If construction of the Project is not complete on 
the Closing Date, the Recipient shall file within thirty (30) days of completion of the Project a certificate, 
in the form provided in EXHIBIT M or as may be updated by the NYSEFC from time to time, certifying 
the Project has been completed in accordance with this Project Finance Agreement. If construction of the 
Project is complete on the Closing Date, the Recipient shall file on or prior to the Closing Date a certificate, 
in the form provided in EXHIBIT M, certifying the Project has been completed in accordance with this 
Project Finance Agreement.

 Inspection. The Recipient shall provide competent and adequate inspection of all 
Project construction by a professional engineer licensed in the State and notify the NYSEFC in advance of 
the date of such inspection in order to provide the NYSEFC with the opportunity to participate in the 
walkthrough and inspection. The Recipient  shall direct such engineer to inspect work necessary for the 
construction of this Project and to determine whether the construction of the Project conforms to the 
approved plans and specifications. At the completion of construction, the engineer shall be required to 
certify to the Recipient, the Agency, and the NYSEFC that the construction of the Project is in accordance 
with the approved plans and specifications or approved amendments thereto in the form provided in 
EXHIBIT M. The Recipient shall cause any work not completed in accordance with approved plans and 
specifications to be remedied, unless such noncompliance is waived in writing by the NYSEFC and the 
Agency.

 Ownership, Operation, and Maintenance. 
 
 Notice of Beginning Operation . Within thirty (30) days following the 
commencement of operation of the Project, the Recipient  shall so notify the Agency and the NYSEFC in

 Operation and Maintenance . The Recipient  shall ensure proper and efficient 
operation and maintenance of this Project satisfactory to the Agency and the NYSEFC and shall retain a 
sufficient number of qualified staff to perform required tests and comply with all other requirements. After 
completion of the Project, the Recipient shall at all times operate the Project, or otherwise cause the Project 
to be operated, properly and in a sound and economical manner and shall maintain, preserve and keep the 
Project, or cause the Project to be maintained, preserved and kept, in good repair, working order and 
condition and shall make, or cause to be made, all necessary and proper repairs, replacements and renewals 
from time to time, so that at all times, the Project is operated properly in a manner consistent with the 
engineering report or  facilities plan for the Project, with this Project Finance Agreement and with the 
requirements of any related State permit. 
 
 Continued Ownership and Operation. Unless authorized by the Commissioner or 
the NYSEFC to cease operations or dispose of the Project, the Recipient shall own the Project and cause 
the Project to be operated and maintained effectively during the term of this Project Finance Agreement. 
Without the approval of the NYSEFC, the Recipient shall not discontinue operation of, or sell or otherwise 
dispose of, the Project, except for portions of the Project sold, or otherwise disposed of, in the course of 
ordinary repair and replacement of obsolete or worn out parts. Except as authorized in wri ting by the 
NYSEFC and the Commissioner, there shall be no alterations to the Project which would materially affect 
the Project in any manner. In addition, no improvements, structures or appurtenances shall be placed, 
constructed or developed on the site of the Project in such a way as to interfere with the express purpose of 
the Project.

 Establishment of Project Accounts. The Recipient shall maintain Project accounts

 Access to Records . The Recipient  shall: (i) permit  the Agency, the State 
Comptroller, or the NYSEFC, or their authorized representatives to review or audit all records relative to 
this Project; (ii) produce or cause to be produced all records relating to any work performed under the terms 
of this Project Finance Agreement for examination at such times as may be designated by the foregoing 
entities; (iii) permit extracts and copies of Project records to be made by the foregoing entities; and (iv) 
promptly fulfill information requests by the foregoing entities. The NYSEFC may require that the Recipient 
provide the NYSEFC with certain financial reports, including without limitation, (i) quarterly cash flow 
reports detailing all revenue receipts for such time period as requested by the NYSEFC; (ii) projected cash 
flow reports for such time periods as requested by the NYSEFC; (iii) annual audited financial statements 
of the Recipient; and (iv) a comprehensive capital budget for the Recipient . If, in the opinion of the 
NYSEFC’s Chief Financial Officer or other authorized officer of the NYSEFC, the information provided 
in such reports and projections indicate a trend which, if not corrected, may result in an Event of Default 
during the term of this Financing, the NYSEFC may demand a corrective action plan from the Recipient  
showing actions that the Recipient  will take to eliminate such risk of Event of Default (the “Corrective 
Action Plan”). Recipient  shall provide the NYSEFC  with a Corrective Action Plan acceptable to the 
NYSEFC within ten (10) Business Days of a request for same. Once the NYSEFC  has requested a 
Corrective Action Plan, timely provision of an acceptable Corrective Action Plan, and adherence thereto, 
may at the election of the NYSEFC in its sole discretion, become conditions to any future disbursements 
under the Financing.  The NYSEFC  may require the Recipient  to provide evidence demonstrating the 
Recipient’s compliance with the terms of this Project Finance Agreement, in a form satisfactory to the 
NYSEFC in its sole discretion.

components at all reasonable times. All contracts of the Recipient related to any portion of the Project must 
contain provisions that permit such access to the Project, and require the contractor to provide proper facilities 
for such access and inspection and shall permit extracts and copies of Project records to be made  by the 
foregoing agents, consultants and representatives.
