 Non-Discrimination Requirements. 
 
Pursuant to New York State Human Rights Law, Article 15-A of the Executive Law, and all other 
State and federal statutory and constitutional non -discrimination provisions, the Recipient  and any 
contractors/subcontractors shall not discriminate against any employee or applicant for employment 
because of race, creed (religion), color, sex (including gender expression), national origin, sexual 
orientation, military status, age, disability, predisposing genetic characteristic, marital status or domestic 
violence victim status, and shall also follow the requirements of the Human Rights Law with regard to non-
discrimination on the basis of prior criminal conviction or prior arrest. Furthermore,  in accordance with 
Section 220-e of the Labor Law, if the Project is the construction, alteration, or repair of any public building 
or public work, the Recipient  and its contractors/subcontractors shall not, by reason of race, creed, color, 
disability, sex, or national origin: (a) discriminate in hiring against any New York citizen who is qualified 
and available to perform the work; or (b) discriminate against or intimidate any employee hired for the 
performance of work under this Project Finance Agreement.

If Recipient has entered into one or more intermunicipal agreements or other contracts relating to 
the Project, Recipient shall not renew, extend or amend such intermunicipal agreement or other contract, 
and shall not enter into any new contract relating to the Project, without notifying the NYSEFC in writing 
and receiving written consent from the NYSEFC. Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, i n the 
event that the term of any intermunicipal agreement relating to the Project is not at least as long as the term 
of this Project Finance Agreement, the Recipient shall, without the NYSEFC’s consent required, renew or 
extend such agreement to keep it in effect for the term of this Project Finance Agreement, and the NYSEFC

may declare an Event of Default if such intermunicipal agreement is terminated, expires, or is not otherwise

 Third-Party Funding.  
 
 Necessary Actions . The Recipient  shall take, in a timely fashion, all actions 
required or necessary to enable it to obtain the full anticipated proceeds of any Third -Party Funding. The 
Recipient shall keep the NYSEFC  apprised of the status of any Third -Party Funding applications and 
immediately notify the NYSEFC of any awarding of Third -Party Funding and the expected timing and 
amount of such Third-Party Funding (including amounts not specifically identified in EXHIBIT E). 
 
 Compliance with Conditions and Requirements.  The Recipient shall comply with 
all stated conditions to any Third -Party Funding commitment, as the same may be amended and 
supplemented, and all applicable present and future eligibility requirements of such Third -Party Funding 
commitment. 
 
 Prompt Notice.  The Recipient shall promptly, and in any event within five (5) 
days after having notice or knowledge thereof, inform the NYSEFC in writing of (i) any anticipated failure 
on its part to meet all eligibility requirements of any Third-Party Funding applied for by the Recipient, (ii) 
any anticipated failure on its part to be qualified to receive any Third-Party Funding proceeds in an amount 
at least equal to such Third-Party Funding commitment, or (iii) the receipt of proceeds of any Third-Party 
Funding. 
 
 Authority to Act. Where the Recipient has failed to submit documentation and/or 
vouchers required to obtain any Third-Party Funding, the Recipient hereby authorizes the NYSEFC, at its 
option, to prepare and submit such documentation and/or vouchers on the Recipient’s behalf. The Recipient 
shall cooperate fully with the NYSEFC by providing necessary data for preparing, executing, and submitting 
such documentation. The Recipient further authorizes the release of any information respecting such assistance 
to the NYSEFC. 
 
 Segregation of Proceeds.   Until Full Repayment, any and all proceeds of any 
Third-Party Funding received by the Recipient on or after the date of the Recipient Financing Instruments 
shall be deposited in a segregated account for use and application solely to pay the principal of the Recipient 
Financing Instruments and shall be so applied, promptly, as and when received, only to the payment of the 
Recipient Financing Instruments, and to no other purpose. 
 
 Pursue Alternate Financing.  If, for any reason, any such Third -Party Funding is 
unavailable, unless it uses other lawfully available funds, the Recipient  will use its best efforts to secure 
alternate financing in an amount sufficient to redeem the Recipient Financing Instruments and the related 
Financing, to the extent of any Third-Party Funding commitment. 
 
 Indemnification.  
 
To the fullest extent permitted by law, the Recipient shall indemnify and defend the NYSEFC, its 
directors, employees, and agents against, and hold each harmless from, any and all losses, claims, damages, 
liabilities and costs of any nature arising out of the execution or delivery of this Project Finance Agreement or 
any agreement or instrument contemplated hereby, the performance by the parties hereto of their respective 
obligations hereunder or thereunder or the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby or thereby, 
or, in the case of the NYSEFC, the administration and enforcement of this Project Finance Agreement and any 
agreement or instrument contemplated hereby (including all such costs and expenses incurred in connection 
with any proceeding under the United States Bankruptcy Code involving Recipient as a debtor thereunder);

provided that such indemnity shall not be available to the extent that such losses, claims, damages, liabilities 
or related expenses are determined by a court of competent jurisdiction by final and nonappealable judgment

agents. 
 
Article VI. BREACH; EVENTS OF DEFAULT AND REMEDIES 
 
 Events of Default. 
 
The occurrence of any of the following shall be considered an Event of Default: 
 
 default in the payment when due of all or any part of the principal of any 
Disbursement (whether at the stated maturity thereof or at any other time provided for in this Project 
Finance Agreement), or default  in the payment when due of any interest, fee or other obligation payable 
hereunder, under any Disbursement, or under any other document contemplated hereby; 
 
 default in the observance or performance of any covenant set forth in Article V or 
of any provision hereunder dealing with the use, disposition, or remittance of the proceeds of 
Disbursements;  
 
 default in the observance or performance of any other provision hereof or of any 
other document contemplated hereby which is not remedied within five (5) Business Days after the earlier 
of (i) the date on which such failure shall first become known  to Recipient or (ii) written notice thereof is 
given to the Recipient by the NYSEFC;  
 
 any representation or warranty made herein or in any other document contemplated 
hereby or in any certificate furnished to the NYSEFC pursuant hereto or thereto or in connection with any 
transaction contemplated hereby or thereby proves untrue in any material respect as of the date of the 
issuance or making or deemed making thereof; 
 
 default shall occur under any other indebtedness issued, assumed or guaranteed by 
the Recipient, or under any indenture, agreement or other instrument under which the same may be issued, 
and such default shall continue for a period of time sufficient to permit the acceleration of the maturity of 
any such indebtedness (whether or not such maturity is in fact accelerated), or any such indebtedness shall 
not be paid when due (whether by demand, lapse of time, acceleration or otherwise); 
 
 a default or breach shall occur under any agreement or contract related to the 
design, construction, or operation and maintenance of the Project by any party thereto; 
 
 the Recipient shall (i) have entered involuntarily against it an order for relief under 
the United States Bankruptcy Code, as amended, (ii) not pay, or admit in writing its inability to pay, its 
debts generally as they become due, (iii) make an assignment for the benef it of creditors, (iv) apply for, 
seek, consent to or acquiesce in, the appointment of a receiver, custodian, trustee, examiner, liquidator or 
similar official for it or any substantial part of its property or the Project, (v) institute any proceeding seeking 
to have entered against it an order for relief under the United States Bankruptcy Code, as amended, to 
adjudicate it insolvent, or seeking dissolution, winding up, liquidation, reorganization, arrangement, 
adjustment or composition of it or its debts un der any law relating to bankruptcy, insolvency or 
reorganization or relief of debtors or fail to file an answer or other pleading denying the material allegations 
Page 93 of 307
 
27 
 
of any such proceeding filed against it, or (vi) take any action in furtherance of any matter described in 
parts (i) through (v) above;  
 
 this Project Finance Agreement or any document contemplated hereby ceases to 
be in full force and effect at any time or for any reason; 
 
 prior to the completion of construction of the Project, the construction of the 
Project is abandoned or work thereon ceases for a period of more than six (6) months for any reason, unless 
Recipient provides a written explanation to the NYSEFC, the content of which is deemed satisfactory in 
the NYSEFC’s sole discretion; and 
 
 a sale, transfer or other disposition of the Project occurs without prior written consent 
of the NYSEFC, and, as appropriate, the Commissioner and State Comptroller. 
 
 Remedies. 
 
Failure by the NYSEFC to exercise, or delay in exercising, any right or remedy under this Article 
VI shall not operate as a waiver of such right or remedy. 
 
 Remedies for Event of Default . Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, the 
NYSEFC may take whatever action at law or in equity may appear necessary or desirable to remedy such 
Event of Default, in addition to the remedies below. The Recipient  shall promptly notify the NYSEFC of 
any Event of Default during the term of this Project Finance Agreement. Failure by the NYSEFC  to 
exercise, or delay in exercising, any right or remedy under this Article VI shall not operate as a waiver of 
such right or remedy. When an Event of Default has occurred and is continuing: 
 
(i) the obligation of the NYSEFC to make further Disbursements pursuant to 
any of the terms hereof and all other obligations of the NYSEFC pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement 
and any related documents shall immediately terminate;  
 
(ii) The NYSEFC may, by written notice to the Recipient, declare the principal 
of and the accrued interest on all outstanding Disbursements to be forthwith due and payable and thereupon all 
outstanding Disbursements, including both principal and interest thereon, shall be and become immediately 
due and payable together with all other amounts payable under this Project Finance Agreement and any related 
documents without further demand, presentment, protest or notice of any kind; and  
 
(iii) The NYSEFC may, by written notice to the Recipient, charge interest at the 
Overdue Interest Rate on the amount then due and payable until such sum has been paid in full to the NYSEFC;  
 
(iv) Pursuant to the NYSEFC Act, if the Recipient fails to make any payment due 
to the NYSEFC pursuant to this Project Finance Agreement  or pursuant to any other undertaking of the 
Recipient (including without limitation the Recipient Financing Instruments)  issued as security  for the 
Financing, the NYSEFC shall recoup such payment from the State Comptroller. In order to do so, the NYSEFC 
shall certify to the State Comptroller, and notify the Chairman of the Assembly Ways & Means Committee, 
the Chairman of the Senate Finance Committee, the Director of the Division of the Budget and the governing 
body of the Recipient that the Recipient has failed to make such payment. Such certificate shall specify the 
exact amount of debt service and surcharge, if applicable, required to satisfy the Recipient’s unpaid obligation. 
Any amounts received by the NYSEFC from the State Comptroller relating to such unpaid amounts shall be 
applied promptly by the NYSEFC to the payment of such unpaid amounts or to reimburse any fund used to 
make such payments on behalf of the Recipient or, if all such payments and reimbursements have been made, 
such amounts received from the State Comptroller shall be applied to pay any other amounts then owed to the 
Page 94 of 307
 
28 
 
NYSEFC by the Recipient. 
 
 Disbursement Holds. 
 
Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, the NYSEFC reserves the right to withhold 
Disbursements for certain Project Costs pursuant to required additional conditions to disbursement set 
forth in Section 4.05. 
 
 Expenses. 
 
The Recipient shall pay to the NYSEFC, as such expenses are incurred, the amount of any expenses 
(including but not limited to the reasonable fees and expenses of the NYSEFC and attorneys representing the 
NYSEFC) incurred as a result of the Recipient’s failure to make any payment on the Recipient Financing 
Instruments when due or other failure to comply with the terms of this Project Finance Agreement or the 
Recipient Financing Instruments. Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, all payments and collections 
received in respect of the Disbursements, by the NYSEFC  after acceleration or the final maturity of the 
Recipient Financing Instruments or termination of the Project Finance Agreement as a result of an Event of 
Default shall be remitted to the NYSEFC and distributed as follows:  
 
 first, to the payment of any outstanding costs and expenses incurred by the 
NYSEFC, in monitoring, verifying, protecting, preserving or enforcing any rights under the Project Finance 
Agreement, the Recipient Financing Instruments, and related documents, and in any event including all costs 
and expenses of a character which the Recipient has agreed to pay the NYSEFC pursuant to this Project 
Finance Agreement; 
 
 second, to the payment of any outstanding interest and fees due under the Project 
Finance Agreement, the Recipient Financing Instruments, and related documents; 
 
 third, to the payment of principal of the Disbursements under the Project Finance 
Agreement, the Recipient Financing Instruments, and related documents; 
 
 fourth, to the repayment of any Grant proceeds disbursed to the Recipient by the 
NYSEFC, if applicable; 
 
 finally, if the NYSEFC receives excess proceeds, to the Recipient or any other 
entity that may be lawfully entitled thereto.

EXHIBIT A PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
 
 
SRF Project No.: C1-5123-12-00 
Recipient: Town of Riverhead 
County: Suffolk 
 
This project consists of construction of sludge processing facilities in the Town of Riverhead. The project 
is defined by the engineering report entitled “Engineering Report Class A Biosolids Upgrade to the 
Riverhead WRRF” dated November 2024 by the engineering firm H2M architects + engineers, as may be 
updated, amended, supplemented, and approved by the New York State Environmental Facilities 
Corporation.

2026 (the “Project Finance Agreement”), between the NYSEFC and the Recipient (the lesser of such amounts 
being hereinafter referred to as the “Unpaid Principal Sum”) plus any and all interest then accrued, late fees and 
other fees and expenses then due , such amount to be paid on the Maturity Date stated above or upon such other 
date as may be established pursuant to the Project Finance Agreement. All Disbursements made by or on behalf 
of the NYSEFC to the Recipient pursuant to the Project Finance Agreement, and all prepayments made on account 
of the Unpaid Principal Sum hereof, interest, fees and other charges shall be recorded by or on behalf of the 
NYSEFC and endorsed on the grid attached hereto in accordance with the terms of the Project Finance Agreement, 
which is hereby made a part hereof. To the extent the “SRF Project No.” includes a “P” suffix, such sub -grid 
prefunds a water infrastructure improvement grant award, provided that the actual costs eligible may not exceed 
the lesser of the “P” sub-grid note amount or the eligible water infrastructure improvement grant award; and the 
disbursement dates under this Note are considered the expenditure dates f or purposes of Section 1.150 -2 of the 
Income Tax Regulations . Recordation of Disbursements, confirmed by a certificate of the Recipient given in 
accordance with the Project Finance Agreement, shall conclusively establish the principal amount outstanding 
hereunder. Interest shall not accrue on said Unpaid Principal Sum prior to the earlier of the Maturity Date or the 
date of earlier redemption of said Unpaid Principal Sum, but in the event of any default in the payment of said 
Unpaid Principal Sum on the earlier of the Maturity Date or the date of earlier redemption, the Recipient promises 
to pay interest on said Unpaid Principal Sum to the Registered Owner named above at the Overdue Interest Rate 
on and after said Maturity Date or date of earlier redemption until sai d Unpaid Principal Sum is paid in full .

Recipient, direct the Recipient to pay any principal of and interest (in respect of overdue principal), if any, on this

principal), if any, on this Note will be paid, without presentment, in funds available on or before the due date and

Ladies and Gentlemen: 
 
 We have examined a record of proceedings relating to the sale and issuance of the $17,633,090.00 E.F.C. 
Clean Water Facility Note (the “Recipient Financing Instrument”) of the Town of Riverhead (the “Recipient”). 
 
 The Recipient Financing Instrument  is issued pursuant to the provisions of the Local Finance Law, 
constituting Chapter 33-A of the Consolidated Laws of the State of New York, and the bond resolution(s) duly 
adopted by the Town of Riverhead  (the “Bond Resolution”) and is being delivered to the New York State 
Environmental Facilities Corporation (the “ NYSEFC”) pursuant to a project finance agreement dated as of 
September 17, 2026 between the NYSEFC and the Recipient (the “Project Finance Agreement”). Any term used 
but not otherwise defined herein shall have the meaning set forth in the Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 The Recipient Financing Instrument is dated September 17, 2026, and matures in the Maximum Principal 
Sum (or such lesser amounts of the Disbursements made thereunder) on the scheduled maturity date set forth 
below: 
 
Maximum Principal Sum: $17,633,090.00 
Scheduled Maturity Date: September 17, 2031 
 
The Recipient Financing Instrument is issued only in fully registered form without interest coupons, in a 
denomination not exceeding the aggregate principal amount of the Recipient Financing Instrument maturing in 
any year. The Recipient Financing Instrument is lettered RST-1. 
 
 The Recipient Financing Instrument is subject to redemption prior to maturity upon the terms set forth in 
the Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 We have reviewed the Constitution and statutes of the State of New York, an executed copy of the Project 
Finance Agreement, certain resolutions of the Recipient, a certified copy of proceedings of the finance board of 
the Recipient and other proofs authorizing and relating to the issuance of the Recipient Financing Instrument, 
including the form of Recipient Financing Instrument, the descriptions of the Project and Existing Indebtedness 
to be refinanced, if any, the Recipient Financing Instrument and other documents to be delivered pursuant to the 
Project Finance Agreement, and such other documents as we have deemed necessary in the rendering of this 
opinion. In rendering the opinions expressed herein we have assumed the accuracy and truthfulness of all public 
records, documents and proceedings, including factual information, expectations and statements contained therein, 
examined by us which have been executed or certified by public officials acting within the scope of their official 
capacities, and have not verified the accuracy or truthfulness thereof. We also have assumed the genuineness of 
the signatures appearing upon such public records, documents and proceedings and the certifications thereof . 
Based upon such review of these documents, it is our opinion that: 
Page 115 of 307
 
H-2 
 
 
 (1) The Project Finance Agreement has been duly authorized, executed and delivered by the 
Recipient. Assuming the due authorization, execution and delivery thereof by the NYSEFC, the Project Finance 
Agreement constitutes a legal, valid, and binding obligation of the Recipient, enforceable in accordance with its 
terms. 
 
 (2) The Recipient Financing Instrument (i) conforms to the descriptions thereof in the Project Finance 
Agreement and (ii) is a valid and legally binding general obligation of the Recipient for which the Recipient has 
validly pledged its faith and credit; and, unless paid from other sources, all the taxable real property within the 
Recipient is subject to the levy of ad valorem real estate taxes to pay the Recipient Financing Instrument  and 
interest thereon subject to applicable statutory limitations. The enforceability of rights or remedies with respect to 
the Project Finance Agreement or the Recipient Financing Instrument may be limited by bankruptcy, insolvency 
or other laws affecting creditors’ rights or remedies heretofore or hereafter enacted. 
 
 (3) We have examined the executed Recipient Financing Instrument and, in our opinion, the form of 
said Recipient Financing Instrument and its execution are regular and proper. 
 
Very truly yours,

K-1 
 
EXHIBIT K PROCEDURES FOR REQUESTS FOR DISBURSEMENTS  
 
PART A 
 
REQUEST FOR DISBURSEMENT SUBMISSION PROCEDURES 
FOR RECIPIENTS 
 
 Part A of this EXHIBIT K sets out the procedures which the Recipient agrees to follow in submitting requests 
for Disbursements of Financing proceeds to finance the planning, design, acquisition, construction and installation of 
any portion of the Project. Section 1 sets out the manner in which requests for Disbursement are to be submitted and 
reviewed. Section 2 sets out the documentation which must be submitted in support of the costs claimed in such 
requests. Capitalized terms are defined in EXHIBIT D to this Project Finance Agreement. 
 
 Section 1. General Requirements. 
 
 All requests by the Recipient for Disbursements of Financing proceeds shall be made by submission to the 
NYSEFC (Attention: Finance Division) of a Supplemental Certificate in substantially the form of EXHIBIT L to this 
Project Finance Agreement in form and substance satisfactory to the NYSEFC  and in each case completed and 
executed by a Chief Fiscal Officer and accompanied by the documentation required pursuant to Section 2 of this Part 
A captioned “Documentation Submission.” 
 
 The NYSEFC agrees to process requests for Disbursement to the Recipient up to the difference between (a) 
the amount of the actual allowable costs incurred by the Recipient on or before the date of submission of the request, 
and (b) any Disbursements already disbursed (including any proceeds disbursed according to the terms of this Project 
Finance Agreement to refinance Existing Indebtedness). Within ten Business Days of its receipt1 of any such request 
for a Disbursement, the NYSEFC shall either (i) make such Disbursement, or (ii) give written notice to the Recipient 
that the NYSEFC is rejecting, correcting, adjusting or withholding such Disbursement in accordance with Article VI 
of this Project Finance Agreement. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the NYSEFC further reserves the right, at any 
time, to demand reimbursement in accordance with Part C of this EXHIBIT K. 
 
 The NYSEFC agrees to send to the Recipient promptly by hand delivery, by telecopy, first class mail, 
electronic mail or overnight delivery service (the choice of method of delivery to be at the discretion of the 
NYSEFC), an acknowledgment of receipt of each such disbursement request.  If a disbursement request is 
approved by the NYSEFC, the NYSEFC will notify the Recipient and the Depository Bank of such approval and 
the amount so approved. 
 
 Section 2. Documentation Submission. 
 
 Unless otherwise agreed to in writing by the NYSEFC or required pursuant to the agreement between EPA 
and the Agency relating to the Revolving Fund, each request for a Disbursement submitted by, or on behalf of, the 
Recipient to the NYSEFC must be accompanied by documentation evidencing the particular Project Costs claimed 
in such request for a Disbursement. The documentation evidencing the particular Project Costs claimed must be in 
form and substance satisfactory to the NYSEFC and in full compliance with the Regulations. Without limiting the 
foregoing, such documentation must, at a minimum, contain specific references, by date and amount, to the particular 
costs claimed in the corresponding request for Disbursement, so that such documentation, and the Project Costs 
evidenced therein, can be reconciled by the NYSEFC with the corresponding request for a Disbursement. In addition, 
 
1 Note that any request for a Disbursement which is received by the NYSEFC after 12:00 noon on any Thursday 
will be deemed to have been received on the following Business Day. 
Page 124 of 307
 
K-2 
 
documentation evidencing the request for Third-Party Funding made to the appropriate parties for release of such 
Third-Party Funding, as applicable, must be submitted with each request. 
 
PART B 
 
WITHHOLDING DISBURSEMENT 
 
 Part B of this EXHIBIT K sets out certain circumstances in which the NYSEFC may reject, correct, adjust, 
or withhold any requests for Disbursements submitted by the Recipient.  The Recipient expressly agrees to the 
terms hereof and further agrees that (i) the rights of the NYSEFC contained herein are in addition to (and not in 
lieu of) any other rights or remedies available to the Agency  and the NYSEFC  under this Project Finance 
Agreement and (ii) nothing contained herein shall be construed to limit the rights of the Agency or the NYSEFC 
to take actions including, but not limited to, administrative enforcement action and actions for breach of contract 
against the Recipient if it fails to carry out its obligations under the Regulations and under this Project Finance 
Agreement during the term thereof. 
 
 Section 1. Rejection. In the absence of a declared Event of Default, a request for a Disbursement may be 
rejected by the NYSEFC if it is: 
 
 (a) submitted without signature; 
 
 (b) submitted under signature of a person other than the Recipient’s duly authorized representative; 
or 
 
 (c) submitted after prior disbursement of all proceeds of this financing. 
 
 The NYSEFC will notify the Recipient and the Depos itory Bank of any request for a  Disbursement so 
rejected, and the reasons therefor. Any request for a Disbursement so rejected must be resubmitted in proper form 
in order to be considered for approval.  If a request for a Disbursement exceeds the balance of the proceeds 
remaining to be disbursed, the request will be treated as if submitted in the amount of the balance so remaining, 
and the NYSEFC will so notify the Recipient. 
 
 Section 2. Correction. A request for a Disbursement containing an apparent mathematical error will 
be corrected by the NYSEFC, after telephonic notification to the Recipient, and will thereafter be treated as if 
submitted in the corrected amount. The NYSEFC will confirm correction of the error, to the Recipient, in writing. 
 
 Section 3. Adjustment. 
 
 (a) If, upon review of a request for a Disbursement, the NYSEFC determines that any portion of the 
costs claimed: 
  
  (i) are ineligible under federal or State law to be financed by the Revolving Fund, or 
 
  (ii) do not constitute costs associated with the Project approved for financing with the proceeds 
of the subject financing, under the terms of this Project Finance Agreement, the NYSEFC will notify the Recipient 
of its determination in writing.  Within thirty (30) days of the date of receipt of such notice, the Recipient shall 
submit additional documentation or evidence to the NYSEFC substantiating the eligibility or approval of such 
costs. 
 
 (b) If, upon review of such additional documentation and evidence, the NYSEFC affirms its original 
determination, the NYSEFC shall be entitled to make adjustments as provided in paragraph (c) below.  If, upon 
Page 125 of 307
 
K-3 
 
review of such additional documentation and evidence, the NYSEFC reverses its determination with respect to 
any such costs, the NYSEFC shall (as applicable) either disburse the appropriate additional sum to the Recipient 
(if, pursuant to paragraph (c), an adjustment was taken out of  the then pending request for a  Disbursement), or 
revise (as required) the adjustment to be made to a succeeding request for a Disbursement (if, pursuant to 
paragraph (c), an adjustment was to be taken out of a succeeding request for a Disbursement). 
 
 (c) If the amount of the costs so determined to be ineligible, or unapproved, is less than the balance 
of the undisbursed proceeds of the related financing (after deducting therefrom the full amount of the pending 
request for a Disbursement), the NYSEFC may accept the pending disbursement and make an adjustment in the 
amount of the ineligible/unapproved costs out of succeeding disbursements.  If the amount of the costs so 
determined to be ineligible, or unapproved, is greater than the balance of undisbursed  proceeds of the related 
financing (after deducting therefrom the full amou nt of the pending request for a  Disbursement), the NYSEFC 
shall be entitled to make an immediate adjustment, and treat the pending request for a Disbursement as if submitted 
in the adjusted amount. 
 
 
PART C 
 
REIMBURSEMENT OF THE NYSEFC 
 
 If the NYSEFC determines that funds disbursed as part of the Financing have been expended by the Recipient 
for costs that are not permissible Project Costs, the NYSEFC shall provide written notice to the Recipient of such 
determination. Such notice shall specify the amount of such funds. 
 
 The Recipient shall, within 30 days after the receipt of such notice, reimburse the NYSEFC in an amount 
equal to the amount of the funds which the NYSEFC has determined were improperly applied. In the event the 
Recipient fails to reimburse the NYSEFC in such amount within 30 days after the receipt of such notice, the Recipient 
shall pay interest on such amount at the Overdue Interest Rate, such interest to begin accruing on the 31st day after 
the Recipient’s receipt of such notice, until such amount is paid.

O-1 
 
EXHIBIT O DAVIS-BACON (DB) PREVAILING WAGE REQUIREMENTS FOR SRF 
RECIPIENTS 
 
1. Applicability of the Davis- Bacon (DB) prevailing wage requirements. 
 
Under the Water Resources Reform and Development Act of 2014 (WRRDA), DB prevailing wage 
requirements apply to the construction, alteration, and repair of treatment works carried out in whole or in 
part with assistance made available by a State water pollu tion control revolving fund. If a Recipient 
encounters a unique situation at a site that presents uncertainties regarding DB applicability, the Recipient 
must discuss the situation with the State Recipient before authorizing work on that site. 
 
2. Obtaining Wage Determinations. 
 
(a) Recipients must obtain the wage determination for the locality in which a covered activity 
subject to DB will take place prior to issuing requests for bids, proposals, quotes or other methods for 
soliciting contracts (solicitation) for activities subje ct to DB. These wage determinations must be 
incorporated into solicitations and any subsequent Contracts. Prime Contracts must contain a provision 
requiring that Subcontractors follow the wage determination incorporated into the prime Contract. 
 
(i) While the solicitation remains open, the Recipient must monitor www.dol.gov weekly 
to ensure that the wage determination contained in the solicitation remains current. Recipients must amend 
the solicitation if Department of Labor (DOL) issues a modification more than 10 days prior to the closing 
date (i.e. bid opening) for the solicitation. If DOL modifies or supersedes the applicable wage determination 
less than 10 days prior to the closing date, the Recipient may request a finding from the State Recipient that 
there is not a reasonable time to notify interested Contractors of the modification of the wage determination. 
The State Recipient will provide a report of its findings to the Recipient. 
 
(ii) If the Recipient does not award the contract within 90 days of the closure of the 
solicitation, any modifications or supersedes DOL makes to the wage determination contained in the 
solicitation shall be effective unless the State Recipient, at the request of the Recipient, obtains an extension 
of the 90 day period from DOL pursuant to 29 CFR 1.6(c)(3)(iv). The Recipient shall monitor www.dol.gov 
on a weekly basis if it does not award  the Contract within 90 days of closure of the solicitation to ensure 
that wage determinations contained in the solicitation remain current. 
 
(b) If the Recipient carries out activity subject to DB by issuing a task order, work assignment or 
similar instrument to an existing Contractor (ordering instrument) rather than by publishing a solicitation, 
the Recipient must insert the appropriate DOL w age determination from www.dol.gov into the ordering 
instrument. 
 
(c) Recipients must review all Subcontracts subject to DB entered into by prime Contractors to 
verify that the prime Contractor has required its Subcontractors to include the applicable wage 
determinations. 
 
(d) As provided in 29 CFR 1.6(f), DOL may issue a revised wage determination applicable to a 
Recipient’s Contract after the award of a Contract or the issuance of an ordering instrument if DOL 
determines that the Recipient has failed to incorporate a wage determination or has used a wage 
determination that clearly does not apply to the Contract or ordering instrument. If this occurs, the Recipient 
must either terminate the Contract or ordering instrument and issue a revised solicitation or ordering 
instrument or incorporate DOL’s wage determination retroactive to the beginning of the Contract or 
ordering instrument by change order. The Recipient’s Contractor must be compensated for any increases in 
wages resulting from the use of DOL’s revised wage determination. 
Page 139 of 307
 
O-2 
 
 
3. Contract and Subcontract provisions. 
 
(a) For Contracts in Excess of $2,000. The Recipient shall insure that the contractors or 
subcontractors shall insert in full in any contract in excess of $2,000 which is entered into for the actual 
construction, alteration and/or repair, including paintin g and decorating, of a treatment work under the 
CWSRF, financed in whole or in part from Federal funds or in accordance with guarantees of a Federal 
agency or financed from funds obtained by pledge of any contract of a Federal agency to make a loan, grant 
or annual contribution (except where a different meaning is expressly indicated), and which is subject to 
the labor standards provisions of any of the acts listed in § 5.1 or FY 2015 Water Resource Reform and 
Development Act 
 
 
4. Compliance Verification. 
 
(a) The Recipient must periodically interview a sufficient number of employees entitled to DB 
prevailing wages (covered employees) to verify that Contractors or Subcontractors are paying the 
appropriate wage rates. As provided in 29 CFR 5.6(a)(6), all interviews must be conducted in confidence. 
The Recipient must use Standard Form 1445 or equivalent documentation to memorialize the interviews.  
Copies of the SF 1445 are available from EPA on request. 
 
(b) The Recipient must establish and follow an interview schedule based on its assessment of the 
risks of noncompliance with DB posed by Contractors or Subcontractors and the duration of the Contract 
or Subcontract. Recipients must increase the frequency o f the interviews if the initial interviews or other 
information indicates that there is a risk that the Contractor or Subcontractor is not complying with DB.  
Recipients must immediately conduct necessary interviews in response to an alleged violation of th e 
prevailing wage requirements. All interviews must be conducted in confidence. 
 
(c) The Recipient must periodically conduct spot checks of a representative sample of weekly 
payroll data to verify that Contractors or Subcontractors are paying the appropriate wage rates. The 
Recipient must establish and follow a spot check schedule base d on its assessment of the risks of 
noncompliance with DB posed by Contractors or Subcontractors and the duration of the Contract or 
Subcontract. At a minimum, the Recipient must spot check payroll data within two weeks of each 
Contractor or Subcontractor’s submission of its initial payroll data and two weeks prior to the completion 
date the Contract or Subcontract.  Recipients must conduct more frequent spot checks if the initial spot 
check or other information indicates that there is a risk that the Contractor or Subcontractor is not complying 
with DB. In addition, during the examinations the Recipient must verify ev idence of fringe benefit plans 
and payments thereunder by Contractors and Subcontractors who claim credit for fringe benefit 
contributions. 
 
(d) The Recipient must periodically review Contractors’ and Subcontractors’ use of apprentices 
and trainees to verify registration and certification with respect to apprenticeship and training programs 
approved by either the U.S Department of Labor or a st ate, as appropriate, and that Contractors and 
Subcontractors are not using disproportionate numbers of laborers, trainees and apprentices. These reviews 
must be conducted in accordance with the schedules for spot checks and interviews described in Item (b)  
and (c) immediately above. 
 
(e) Upon the request of EFC, the Recipient must provide EFC with a written certification indicating 
whether or not the project is in compliance with the requirements of 29 CFR 5.5(a)(1) based on the most 
recent payroll copies from Contractors/Subcontractors for the specified week. 
 
Page 140 of 307
 
O-3 
 
(f) Recipients must immediately report potential violations of the DB prevailing wage requirements 
to the EPA DB contact listed above and to the appropriate DOL Wage and Hour District Office listed at 
www.dol.gov. 
 
5. Additional requirements for Recipients that are not governmental entities. 
 
Recipients that are not governmental entities must submit their proposed DB wage determinations to the 
State Recipient for approval prior to including the wage determinations in any solicitation, Contract or 
issuing task orders, work assignments, or simila r instruments to existing Contractors, as well as ordering 
instruments unless subsequently directed otherwise by the State Recipient award official as identified 
below. 
 
Recipients must obtain proposed wage determinations for specific localities at www.dol.gov. After the 
Recipient obtains its proposed wage determination, it must submit the wage determination to the State 
Recipient award official at: Director, Engineering and Program Management, New York State 
Environmental Facilities Corporation, at 518-402-7396.

CERTIFICATION REGARDING LOBBYING FOR CONTRACTS, GRANTS, LOANS, AND 
COOPERATIVE AGREEMENTS 
40 CFR PART 34 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
SRF Project No.: C1-5123-12-00 
 
 
The undersigned certifies, to the best of his or her knowledge and belief, that: 
 
(1) No Federal appropriated funds have been paid or will be paid, by or on behalf of the 
undersigned, to any person for influencing or attempting to influence an officer or 
employee of any agency, a Member of Congress, an officer or employee of Congress, or 
an employee of a Member of Congress in connection with the awarding of any Federal 
contract, the making of any Federal grant, the making of any Federal loan, the entering into 
of any cooperative agreement, and the extension, continuation, renewal, amendment,  or 
modification of any Federal contract, grant, loan, or cooperative agreement. 
 
(2) If any funds other than Federal appropriated funds have been paid or will be paid to any 
person for influencing or attempting to influence an officer or employee of any agency, a 
Member of Congress, an officer or employee of Congress, or an employee of a Member of 
Congress in connection with this Federal contract, grant, loan, or cooperative agreement, 
the undersigned shall complete and submit Standard Form -LLL, “Disclosure Form to 
Report Lobbying,” in accordance with its instructions. 
 
(3) The undersigned shall require that the language of this certification be included in the 
award documents for all sub awards at all tiers (including subcontracts, sub grants, and 
contracts under grants, loans, and cooperative agreements) and that all sub-recipients shall 
certify and disclose accordingly. 
 
This certification is a material representation of fact upon which reliance was placed when this transaction 
was made or entered into. Submission of this certification is a prerequisite for making or entering into this 
transaction imposed by section 1352, title 31 , U.S. Code. Any person who fails to file the required 
certification shall be subject to a civil penalty of not less than $10,000 and not more than $100,000 for each 
such failure.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-858 
 
AUTHORIZES SUBMISSION OF LETTER OF INTENT AND EXECUTION OF CONTRACT BY THE 
SUPERVISOR FOR OPERATION AND MAINTENANCE GRANT PROGRAM TO NEW YORK STATE 
ENVIRONMENTAL FACILITIES CORPORATION (EFC) 
 
Councilman Kern offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Merrifield 
 
          WHEREAS, the State of New York Environmental Facilities Corporation (EFC) provides 
assistance to municipalities that provide boat pumpout facilities for marine waste holding tanks 
and portable toilets; and           
 
          WHEREAS, funding is available for up to 75%, with a maximum annual grant of $5,000 
for pumpout boats and $2,000 for stationary facilities; and           
 
          WHEREAS, it is the policy of the Riverhead Town Board to encourage those practices 
that minimize hazardous influences on the Peconic Estuary system; and           
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead is eligible for up to $14,000 in reimbursement for the 
2026 season.           
 
          THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, by the Riverhead Town Board authorizes the 
Supervisor to submit the letter of intent and execute a contract with EFC.           
 
          THEREFORE, BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk shall provide a copy of 
this resolution to the Community Development Department and the Police Department. 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilman Robert Kern 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield 
AYES: None 
NAYS: None

TB Resolution 2026-859 
 
AUTHORIZES COMMENCEMENT OF ACTION - 47 BAYSIDE AVENUE 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town Board has determined that the property situated at 47 Bayside 
Avenue, Jamesport, New York, is being used and occupied in violation of various sections of the 
Code of the Town of Riverhead; and 
  
WHEREAS, the Office of the Town Attorney has recommended and requested that the 
Town Board authorize the Town Attorney to commence a proceeding in the Supreme Court, 
County of Suffolk, against the owners of 47 Bayside Avenue, Jamesport, New York. 
  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead 
hereby authorizes the commencement of a proceeding in the Supreme Court, County of Suffolk 
against the owners, occupants and/or tenants of 47 Bayside Avenue, Jamesport, New York, and 
it is left to the discretion of the Town Attorney as to whether to seek injunctive relief, civil 
penalties or any other appropriate form of relief; and be it further, 
  
       RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a Certified copy of this 
resolution to the Office of the Town Attorney and the Accounting Department; and be it further,  
  
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
 
THE VOTE

MOVER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Joann Waski 
AYES: None

TB Resolution 2026-860 
 
RATIFIES AUTHORIZATION FOR THE RIVERHEAD POLICE DEPARTMENT TO APPLY FOR GRANT 
FUNDING THROUGH THE DEPARTMENT OF JUSTICE'S OFFICE OF COMMUNITY ORIENTED 
POLICING SERVICES TECHNOLOGY AND EQUIPMENT PROGRAM 
 
Councilwoman Waski offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Rothwell 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead Police Department is seeking to purchase technology 
to extend and enhance the contact of body-worn cameras; and 
 
          WHEREAS, the Department of Justice’s Office of Community Oriented Police Services 
(COPS), through the FY 2026 COPS Technology and Equipment Program (TEP) and New York 
State LETECH grant, provides funding for law enforcement technology and equipment to 
enhance crime prevention, response, and community policing; and 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead Police Department is seeking funding in the amount 
of $478,000 to update and enhance existing equipment and technology; and 
 
          WHEREAS, there is no match required from the Town of Riverhead if this funding is 
awarded; and 
 
          NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Riverhead Town Board ratifies 
authorization for the submission of the FY 2026 COPS Technology and Equipment Program 
(TEP) to the Department of Justice; and 
 
          BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the filing of the grant application is authorized, and the 
Supervisor is hereby authorized to sign any and all documents necessary in furtherance of this 
application as well as any contracts and related forms or documents that are required by the 
granting entity should the grant be awarded; and 
 
          BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that upon notification of award, the Town of Riverhead 
Financial Administrator has the authority to establish the appropriate budget for revenue and 
expenditure; and 
 
          BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk forward copies of this resolution to the 
Riverhead Police Department; and 
 
          BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a 
copy of this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of the 
same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
PUBLIC NOTICE 
 
PLEASE TAKE NOTICE, that a public hearing will be held before the Town 
Board of the Town of Riverhead, at 4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York on the 
6th day of October, 2026 at or about 2:15 p.m. to consider a proposed local law to 
amend Chapter 289 of the Riverhead Town Code titled “Vehicles, Traffic and Parking 
Regulations”, Article II “Traffic Regulations”. 
 
The intent of the proposed legislation is to memorialize an already existing stop 
intersection between Doctors Path and County Road 43 (Northville Turnpike).  
 
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE that a copy of the entire text of the proposed 
local law and the provisions of the existing law to be amended may be reviewed at the 
Office of the Town Clerk, 4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York 11901, between 
the hours of 8:30 a.m. and 4:30 p.m., Monday through Friday and can be accessed on 
the Town of Riverhead website at: www.townofriverheadny.gov under the Agenda and 
Minutes for the September 15, 2026 Town Board Meeting. 
 
Dated: Riverhead, New York 
September 15, 2026 
 
BY THE ORDER OF THE TOWN BOARD 
   OF THE TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
JAMES M. WOOTEN, Town Clerk

TB Resolution 2026-862 
 
AUTHORIZES THE TOWN CLERK TO PUBLISH AND POST NOTICE FOR PUBLIC HEARING 
REGARDING US HUD COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT BLOCK GRANT ("CDBG") 2027 FUNDS 
 
Councilman Kern offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Merrifield 
 
         WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead participates in the Suffolk County Consortium to 
receive Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) funds from the United States Department 
of Housing and Urban Development (US HUD) for the benefit of low- and moderate-income 
persons and for the prevention of slum and blight; and 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead has an ongoing CDBG Program and plans to prepare 
an application for CDBG Funds for Fiscal Year 2027 with an estimated allocation of up to 
$275,000; and  
 
          WHEREAS, the Town wishes to solicit comments from the public with regard to the 
development of said application.  
 
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Riverhead Town Board hereby 
authorizes the Town Clerk to publish and post the attached notice of a public hearing in the 
September 24, 2026 issue of the Riverhead News Review, announcing a public hearing to be 
held Tuesday October 6, 2026 at 2:00 p.m. at Riverhead Town Hall, 4 West Second Street, 
Riverhead, NY 11901; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, the commencement of the mandatory 30-day written comment period shall 
begin on the publication date of September 24, 2026 and will end on October 24, 2026; and be it 
further, 
 
         RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this resolution to 
Community Development and Rosemarie Pforr, pforr@suffolkcountyny.gov; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
PUBLIC NOTICE 
 
 
PUBLIC INPUT/REQUEST FOR FUNDING ON COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT PROGRAM 
ANNOUNCEMENT OF PUBLIC HEARING 
 
 
 PLEASE TAKE NOTICE the Town of Riverhead expects to apply for up to  $275,000 in Federal 
Community Development Block Grant Funds for FY2027.  These funds may be used, and have been previously 
used, for a variety of projects that benefit low - and moderate -income persons, aid in the prevention or 
elimination of slums or blight and/or meet a need having a particular urgency. 
 
 Examples of eligible activities include: 
   
Housing Rehabilitation 
  Elimination of Physical Barriers to the Handicapped 
  Public Facilities and Improvements 
  Street Improvements 
  Public Service Activities 
   
Town resident s are invited to attend this publ ic hearing on Tuesday, October 6, 2026 at 2:00 p.m. at 
Riverhead Town Hall, 4 West Second Street , Riverhead, New York,  11901 to provide public input and/or 
request funding on community development needs and/or submit wri tten comments to Riverhead Town H all, 
Community Development Department, 4 West Second Street , Riverhead, New York 11901 by Octo ber 24, 
2026. 
 
Dated:  September 15, 2026              BY ORDER OF THE TOWN BOARD 
       TOWN OF RIVERHEAD, NEW YORK 
 
       JAMES WOOTEN, TOWN CLERK

Councilwoman Waski offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Rothwell 
 
 
WHEREAS, on September 4, 2026, DMS Apex Holdings LLC., submitted a Chapter 255 
Application for the purpose of conducting an event entitled “Skip Barber Advanced Autocross” at 
Calverton Enterprise Park, Calverton, New York, on September 12th 2026 between the hours of 
7:00am to 5:00pm without a rain date; and 
 
WHEREAS, the applicant has paid the requisite Chapter 255 Application fee of $375.00; 
and 
 
WHEREAS, a certificate of insurance naming the Town of Riverhead as an additional 
insured has been received; and  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead 
hereby has classified the action as a Type II under SEQRA NYCRR 617.5 C (21) and requiring 
no further environmental review; and be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that any tent installations, including the obtainment of any necessary tent 
permits, and any all electric shall comply with the applicable provisions of the Building and Fire 
Code of New York State, the National Electrical Code and National Fire Protection Association 
102 (Tents & Membrane Structures); and be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that should it be determined that police and or fire marshal personnel and 
vehicle usage become necessary for this event, then in that instance, DMS Apex Holdings LLC 
agrees to pay the costs associated with same, which shall become due and payable upon 
delivery of an invoice to them for those additional costs; and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that this approval is subject to the provisions of Riverhead Town Code 
Chapter 301-251 G (7) entitled “Signs” and any other section of the Riverhead Town Code that 
may pertain to this event; and be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that the Outdoor Safety Plan submitted with the applicant’s chapter 255 
application is subject to the review and approval of the Riverhead Fire Marshal; and be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that the site diagram of the event must be submitted and is subject to the 
review and approval of the Riverhead Fire Marshal; and be it further, 
  
RESOLVED, that the Chapter 255 Application of DMS Apex Holdings LLC for the 
purpose of conducting an event entitled “Skip Barber Advanced Autocross” at Calverton 
Enterprise Park, Calverton, New York, on the aforesaid dates and times, is hereby approved; 
and be it further,

RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this resolution 
to Chief of Police, Edward Frost, Chief Fire Marshal, Andrew Smith and Ben Rothman 
ben.rothman@skipbarber.com, and be it further,  
 
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a copy of same may be obtained 
from the Office of the Town Clerk.

9/15/2026 IX.33.  
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-873 
 
AUTHORIZES DESIGNATED ALCOHOL SERVICE VENDOR TO SERVE ALCOHOL AT THE RIVERHEAD 
CHAMBER OF COMMERCE “CONCERTS IN THE PARK” 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, the Riverhead Town Board approved a Chapter 255 special event permit by 
Resolution #2026-670 adopted on July 7, 2026 for the Riverhead Chamber of Commerce to 
conduct a “CONCERTS IN THE PARK” event for Monday, October 12, 2026 from 6:00 p.m. to 
9:00 p.m., at Grangebel Park located at Peconic Avenue, Riverhead, New York 11901 (depicted 
on Schedule A) annexed hereto, from the Town of Riverhead, as outlined on the attached map 
(Schedule “A”); and 
  
WHEREAS, Riverhead Chamber of Commerce has requested permission to permit 
alcohol service from a designated vendor within the licensed premises at the afore-mentioned 
location, dates and time; and 
  
WHEREAS, Riverhead Chamber of Commerce has designated the following alcohol 
service vendor regarding participation in the “Concerts in the Park” event: 
  
TIPPING COW DISTILLERY LLC. d/b/a TWISTED COW DISTILLERY  
13 Hewitt Square 
East Northport, NY 11731 
  
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town of Riverhead approves 
participation of the above-referenced alcohol service vendors regarding alcohol service at the 
“Concerts in the Park” event at the afore-mentioned location, dates and time, subject to the 
following conditions: 
  
1. The participating alcohol service vendor shall comply with New York State Alcohol 
Beverage Control Laws, NYS Liquor Authority Rules and Regulations, NYS Governor 
Executive Orders, including successive Orders, the Town of Riverhead Alcohol Policy and 
Town Code provisions, as applicable. 
  
2. The participating alcohol service vendor shall execute a license agreement in a form to be 
approved by the Town Attorney’s Office. 
  
3. The participating alcohol service vendor shall procure a general liability insurance policy for 
the subject activity naming the Town of Riverhead, Town of Riverhead Business 
Improvement District and Riverhead Business Improvement District Management 
Association, Inc., as “additional insureds” on a primary and non-contributory basis, with 
policy limits of at least $1,000,000 per occurrence/ $2,000,000 aggregate, including 
food/product service liability protection, if applicable. In addition to the above referenced 
Page 211 of 307
insurance requirements, alcohol service vendors shall also procure an additional insurance 
liability policy regarding alcohol service liability with policy limits of at least $1,000,000.00 
dollars per occurrence/$2,000,000.00 dollars aggregate which shall name the above-
referenced parties as additional insureds also on a primary and non-contributory basis. 
  
4.  Alcohol service shall also be subject to the following conditions: 
  
1. All alcohol servers must be TIPS (Training for Intervention Procedures) approved by 
New York State. 
2. Alcohol service and consumption shall be limited to the authorized and approved alcohol 
service vendor as limited to the licensed premises addressed above (as delineated on the 
Concerts in the Park map). 
3. Alcohol service shall be solely limited to beer, cider, wine and/or champagne.  
4. Alcohol service shall be limited to two (2) drinks per age-appropriate -customer at a 
time. 
5. Licensee shall possess and provide, prior to participation, a valid New York State Liquor 
Authority catering permit, or other appropriate permit, regarding off-premises alcohol 
service and comply with all applicable permit requirements including all other applicable 
municipal laws, rules and regulations, including service hours. 
6. Alcohol service shall be served in clear plastic cups and contain an identifying symbol 
and/or logo uniquely identifying the vendor. Each such cup shall be limited in size to 12 
oz. for beer/cider; 5 oz. for wine/champagne. 
7. Alcohol servers shall be required to hand-stamp age-appropriate alcohol patrons prior to 
alcohol service. 
8. Such other conditions as may be more fully delineated in the alcohol vendor license 
agreement and Town alcohol policy; 
9. Licensee shall retain a private security company at its own cost and expense to monitor 
alcohol consumption activity. 
  
 and be it further,  
 
RESOLVED, that the Riverhead Town Code section 205-3, which prohibits the 
consumption of any alcoholic beverages in any public place within the Town of Riverhead and 
also prohibits the possession of any open or unsealed container of an alcoholic beverage while 
in a public place for the purposes of consumption, is hereby suspended for enforcement 
purposes solely regarding the “Concerts in the Park” event on Monday, 6:00 p.m. to 9:00 p.m., 
subject to the above-stated requirements, solely limited to the licensed premises as delineated 
on the attached map (Schedule “A”) and subject to the New York State Alcohol Beverage 
Control Law and NYS Liquor Authority Rules and Regulations; and it be further,  
  
RESOLVED, that the Town of Riverhead waives the Town of Riverhead alcohol policy 
requirement regarding submission of a security/damage deposit by participating alcohol service 
vendors; and be it further, 
  
RESOLVED, that the Town Supervisor is authorized to execute license agreements with 
participating alcohol service vendors in a form to be approved by the Town Attorney’s Office; 
and be it further, 
  
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a Certified copy of this 
resolution to Chief Ed Frost, Riverhead Town Police Department; Andrew Smith, Chief Fire 
Page 212 of 307
Marshal, the Engineering Department and the Town Attorney’s Office; and be it further,  
  
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.   
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilman Kenneth Rothwell 
SECONDER: Councilman Robert Kern 
AYES: None 
NAYS: None

9/15/2026 IX.35.  
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-875 
 
ADOPTS THE SUFFOLK COUNTY 2026 HAZARD MITIGATION PLAN 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead recognizes the threat that natural hazards pose to people 
and property within Riverhead Town; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead has participated in the preparation and review of a 
multi-hazard mitigation plan, hereby known as the Suffolk County 2026 Hazard Mitigation Plan 
in accordance with the Disaster Mitigation Act of 2000; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Suffolk County 2026 Hazard Mitigation Plan identifies mitigation goals 
and actions to reduce or eliminate long-term risk to people and property in the Town of 
Riverhead from the impacts of future hazards and disasters; and 
 
WHEREAS, adoption by the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead demonstrates their 
commitment to hazard mitigation and achieving the goals outlined in the Suffolk County 2026 
Hazard Mitigation Plan. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead 
hereby adopts the Suffolk County 2026 Hazard Mitigation Plan, and this plan, approved by the 
community, may be edited or amended after submission for review, but will not require the 
community to re-adopt any further iterations, and the foregoing applies only to this specific plan 
and does not absolve the community from updating the plan in 5 years; and be it further, 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this resolution to 
the Police Department, the Office of the Fire Marshal and the Office of the Town Attorney; and 
be it further, 
 
           RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Joann Waski 
AYES: None 
NAYS: None

9/15/2026 IX.37.  
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
TB Resolution 2026-877 
 
BUDGET ADJUSTMENT FOR 2026 RECORDS MANAGEMENT AND DIGITAL CONVERSION 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town of Riverhead needs to modernize their records management for 
the Building Department and convert records to digital format; and 
 
WHEREAS, the I.T. Department received proposals for the modernization of records with 
an estimated total cost of $1,243,621. A budget transfer is needed from the General Fund’s 
available fund balance to cover the estimated current year costs of $300,000 for professional 
services to implement digitization as well as Laserfiche licensing. Now, therefore be it 
 
RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby authorized to establish the following 
budget adjustments; 
  
 FROM                TO 
 
A01-9999-000-00000-0 Appropriated Fund Balance          $300,000.00 
  
A01-1-1680-436-210-00000 I.T. – Prof Svcs – Other                         $265,000.00 
 
A01-1-1680-430-000-00000 I.T. – License Support & Maintenance   $ 35,000.00 
 
And be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this resolution 
to finance@townofriverheadny.gov , the I.T. Department, the Building Department and the 
Purchasing Department, and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: 
 
MOVER: Councilman Kenneth Rothwell 
SECONDER: Councilman Robert Kern 
AYES: None 
NAYS: None
